## Phase 9 — Report Editor Rich-Text & AI Panel Upgrade

**Objective:** Upgrade `SectionedReportEditor.jsx` from plain textareas to genuine rich-text editing with full section management, and implement the remaining 6 of 7 spec'd AI panel functions.

**Reason this phase is required:** Item 27 (`PARTIALLY COMPLETE`) — no formatting controls, no add/delete/reorder sections, and only 1 of 7 AI functions (generic "Suggest") exists.

**Scope:** Frontend editor component (likely a rich-text library integration), backend AI-panel endpoints for the additional functions.

**Exact tasks:**
1. **OPEN QUESTION:** Choose a rich-text editing approach — a lightweight library (e.g. TipTap/Slate) vs. a structured-block editor. This is a real technical decision with bundle-size/complexity tradeoffs; flag for a short technical spike before committing.
2. Add formatting controls: bold/italic/underline/heading levels/bullets/numbering/tables/insert photo+photo grid/caption/page break/undo-redo.
3. Add section management: add section, delete section, reorder (drag-and-drop or up/down controls), rename (already exists).
4. Reconcile the AI-generated default section set with the spec's list (Report Info, Claim Info, Insured Info, Property Info, Inspection Details, Inspection Overview, Area Observations, Photo Documentation, Additional Notes) — update `aiService.js`'s report-generation prompt's section structure.
5. Implement the remaining 6 AI panel functions as distinct backend prompts/endpoints: Improve Writing, Shorten, Expand, Rewrite Professionally, Check Consistency, Check Missing Information, Review Photo Documentation — all following the existing Accept/Reject, never-auto-overwrite pattern.

**Files/components likely affected:** `frontend/src/components/SectionedReportEditor.jsx`, `backend/routes/reports.js` (`/sections/suggest` and new sibling endpoints), `backend/services/aiService.js`.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** None hard-blocking; benefits from Phase 8 (photo data) for the "Review Photo Documentation" function specifically.

**Expected output:** A genuinely capable report editor matching the spec's 3-pane vision.

**Acceptance criteria:** All 6 new AI functions work end-to-end with Accept/Reject; sections can be added/removed/reordered without data loss; rich-text formatting persists correctly through save/reload/export.

**Testing/verification steps:** Full editor pass — format text, reorder sections, run each of the 7 AI functions, save, reload, confirm formatting/order survived; export to PDF/DOCX and confirm formatting carries through.

**Risks/considerations:** Rich-text content must still export cleanly to PDF/DOCX/HTML (Phase 11 territory) — coordinate the internal content representation (e.g. structured JSON vs. HTML vs. Markdown) so both phases agree on a single source of truth.

**Amendment (2026-08-16 full-spec audit) — 1 additional task:**
6. **"Regenerate Section" as its own distinct workflow, not a rename of the existing "Suggest" function.** Verified: the existing `POST /:id/sections/suggest` + `SectionedReportEditor.jsx` flow takes only `title`/`body` (no open-ended instruction field) and shows the suggestion in a stacked accept/reject panel below the original text. The spec (§49) wants a specific, distinct UX: a "Regenerate Section" menu item opens a modal with an open-ended textarea ("What would you like to change?"), then a Cancel/Regenerate choice, then a comparison view *before* replacing the section content. Decide whether this becomes an 8th AI panel function alongside the existing one, or whether "Suggest" is extended in place to add the missing instruction field + comparison view (recommend the latter — avoids two near-identical AI-rewrite entry points confusing users).

**Status:** ✅ **Completed (2026-08-16)** — implemented per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 9 only"), ahead of Phase 30 (routing migration). See "Implementation summary" below for why this was safe to do out of the table's stated dependency order, and "Client Resolutions" for the two open technical decisions (rich-text library, Regenerate-vs-Suggest UX) this session made.

**Client/engineering decisions made this session (both previously open questions):**
- **Rich-text editor library (Open Question #4): TipTap 2 (`@tiptap/react` + `@tiptap/starter-kit` + `@tiptap/extension-underline`/`table`/`table-row`/`table-header`/`table-cell`/`placeholder`).** Chosen over Slate/a hand-rolled contentEditable for three reasons: (a) a real, maintained ProseMirror-based schema — arbitrary/unsupported HTML (e.g. a pasted `<script>`) is dropped by the parser rather than needing separate sanitization; (b) first-class custom-node support (`Node.create()` + `addNodeView()`) for the report-specific `pageBreak`/`reportPhoto`/`reportPhotoGrid` nodes without fighting the library; (c) an actively maintained v2 release with a stable React binding. Bundle-size cost is real (`SectionedReportEditor` chunk grew to ~390KB / ~117KB gzipped — see the frontend build's own chunk-size warning) but was accepted as the reasonable price of a genuine rich editor.
- **"Regenerate Section" implementation (task 6 above): extended `POST /:id/sections/suggest` in place** (adds an optional `instructions` field) rather than adding an 8th, near-duplicate `/sections/assist` action — matches the amendment's own recommendation. The frontend surfaces it as a visually distinct "Regenerate Section" button (separate from the "FLACRON Tools" AI panel) that opens its own instructions → loading → comparison → Approve/Cancel modal, per the spec's exact workflow.

**Persistent content format decision (not a prior open question, but the single biggest architectural choice this phase required):** `report.content` **stays a single Markdown string** — no Firestore schema change. This was the key risk-reduction call: it means legacy reports, version history (`recordVersion`), `PUT /:id`, `POST /:id/approve`, `POST /:id/export`, and the report-sharing/webhook code all keep working completely unchanged. The editor's `SectionedReportEditor` component still receives `value` (markdown) and calls `onChange(markdownString)` — its external contract to `Dashboard.jsx`/`EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` is **byte-for-byte identical to before Phase 9**, so neither of those files needed to change at all. Internally, each section's body round-trips through a small extended-Markdown dialect (documented in `backend/utils/richContent.js` and mirrored in `frontend/src/utils/richContent.js`):
- `**bold**` / `*italic*` (unchanged) / `++underline++` (new) — a linear, independent-toggle inline tokenizer (not a recursive parser) that correctly handles any nesting/order and never mistakes an underscore inside a word (e.g. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) for emphasis, since underline deliberately does not use `_..._`. Literal `*`/`+` characters a user types are escaped (`\*`/`\+`) on save and un-escaped on load, so round-tripping never corrupts real content into accidental formatting.
- `1. item` numbered lists (new, alongside the existing `- `/`* ` bullets).
- `{{page-break}}` — a literal line token, rendered as a real page break in PDF (`addPage()`) and DOCX (`<w:br w:type="page"/>`), a styled divider in HTML/the editor.
- `![[photo:PHOTO_ID|Caption]]` (single photo) and a `[[photos cols=N]]` / `photo:ID|caption` (repeated) / `[[/photos]]` fenced block (photo grid) — both resolve **only** against the report's own `photos` array (or legacy `imagePaths` index), server-side, at export time. There is no client-supplied-URL path at all for images, so there's no SSRF/arbitrary-image/src-injection surface.

**Exact tasks — implementation summary:**
1. ✅ Rich-text editor: TipTap-based `SectionedReportEditor.jsx` (full rewrite, same external props). Toolbar: Bold, Italic, Underline, Sub-heading (H3), Bullet list, Numbered list, Insert table, Insert photo, Insert photo grid, Insert page break, Undo, Redo — all live-verified in a real browser (see Testing below), including Undo/Redo correctness.
2. ✅ Formatting controls — see above; table insert uses TipTap's own `Table`/`TableRow`/`TableHeader`/`TableCell` extensions; photo/photo-grid/page-break are three small custom nodes in `frontend/src/components/reportEditor/extensions.js` (schema-constrained — atoms with fixed attrs only, so there is no path for arbitrary HTML to enter the document through them). "Caption" is implemented as click-to-edit on a photo node's caption line (a small modal), not a separate always-visible toolbar button.
3. ✅ Section management: Add Section (button, inserts a blank `## New Section`), Delete Section (confirm dialog, refuses to leave zero sections), Rename (existing title input, unchanged UX), Reorder (Move Up/Down arrow buttons — chosen over drag-and-drop for reliability/accessibility; explicitly allowed by this task's own wording). All four operations go through one `emit()` path that derives the next `sections` array from the current one and calls `onChange` once — deliberately *not* `setSections(prev => ...)` with side effects inside the updater, since React 18 StrictMode double-invokes updater functions in dev and that would have double-fired `onChange` (a real bug caught and fixed during implementation, before any live testing).
4. ✅ Default section structure reconciled with the spec's list (Report Info, Claim Info & Insured Info, Property Info, Inspection Details & Overview, Area Observations, Photo Documentation, Additional Notes) in `aiService.js`'s `buildReportPrompt()` — **titles renamed, numbering and content preserved**: `SECTION 1: REPORT INFO` (was "Report Header"), `SECTION 2: CLAIM INFO & INSURED INFO` (was "Insured Information"), `SECTION 3: PROPERTY INFO`, `SECTION 4: INSPECTION DETAILS & OVERVIEW`, `SECTION 5: AREA OBSERVATIONS (DAMAGE ASSESSMENT)`, `SECTION 6: SCOPE OF WORK` (unchanged — not in the spec's 9-item list but load-bearing, existing, Golden-Rule-relevant content, kept rather than deleted), `SECTION 7: PRELIMINARY ESTIMATED COSTS` (unchanged — title and numbering are regex-anchored by Phase 1's `ensureLossSummary` safety net), `SECTION 8: PHOTO DOCUMENTATION` (was "Supporting Documentation" — exact match to the spec, and already where Phase 8's `insertPhotoObservations`/`insertNoPhotoDisclaimer` hook in), `SECTION 9: ADDITIONAL NOTES & CONCLUSION (ITEMS FOR ADJUSTER REVIEW)` (was "Conclusion..." — regex-anchored by `ensureConclusion`). **Deliberate engineering call:** did not literally collapse to exactly 9 sections 1:1 with the spec's list, because that would have required deleting Scope of Work/Cost Estimate/Conclusion content that is core, already-tested, and Golden-Rule #2-relevant (cautious-cost-estimate framing, the adjuster-review conclusion). Every one of the spec's 9 names is now either its own section or folded into a title that names it explicitly (e.g. "Claim Info & Insured Info"), so nothing from the spec's list is silently missing — but the full original 9-section structure (with its cost table and conclusion) is preserved rather than replaced. All existing Phase 1/8 safety nets (`ensureLossSummary`, `ensureConclusion`, `insertNoPhotoDisclaimer`, `insertPhotoObservations`) needed zero regex changes since they anchor on `SECTION\s*N`, not the title text after it. The PDF's table-of-contents labels and the PDF/DOCX "Report Header/Insured Information → bold label, no bullet" field-list special-casing were updated to match the new titles.
5. ✅ All 7 FLACRON ENGINE writing-assistance functions implemented as a new `POST /:id/sections/assist` endpoint (`action` ∈ `improve`/`shorten`/`expand`/`rewrite_professional`/`check_consistency`/`check_missing_info`/`review_photos`) plus the existing `/sections/suggest` extended for Regenerate Section (task 6) — **8 total AI entry points**, matching this phase's own task list's minor "remaining 6" vs. 7-named-functions discrepancy by simply implementing every named function. `check_consistency` is given the full report content for cross-section comparison; `review_photos` is given a compact per-photo reviewed-state summary (location/category/reviewer status/observation, capped at 60 photos) built server-side from `report.photos` — never raw client input. All 7 share one prompt-building function (`buildAssistPrompt`) and the same Golden Rule #2 guardrail block, and all return a `suggestion` for the editor's uniform Apply/Discard panel — including the two "check" functions, which return the *original* section text with a deterministic `AI Review Notes — ...:` paragraph appended, so Apply/Discard stays one consistent UI pattern across all 7 instead of a special-cased "read-only findings" shape for two of them.
6. ✅ Regenerate Section — see "Client/engineering decisions" above.

**Sanitization against unsafe HTML/content:** implemented as defense-in-depth rather than a stripping/escaping pass on `content` itself, because the existing PDF/DOCX/HTML export code already neutralizes hostile text at render time (`escapeXml`/`escapeHtml`/PDFKit's inherently-textual `.text()` calls) — confirmed still true by the pre-existing `backend/test/exports.test.js` hostile-content fixture, which specifically expects a literal `<script>` to survive as **inert escaped text**, not be stripped (a stripping sanitizer would have broken that already-passing, intentional test). What Phase 9 actually adds:
- `sanitizeReportContent()`/`sanitizeInstructions()` (`backend/utils/richContent.js`) — bound size (300,000 chars for content, 2,000 for the Regenerate Section `instructions` field) and strip non-printable control characters, applied on every client-supplied-content write path (`PUT /:id`, `POST /:id/approve`). Live-verified: a 400,000-character `PUT /:id` was accepted (not a 500) and the persisted Firestore document's `content` was confirmed capped at exactly 300,000 characters.
- The rich editor's own security property, by construction: `markdownToDoc()` (frontend `richContent.js`) never calls `editor.commands.setContent(htmlString)` with HTML parsing on untrusted text — every node is built as a plain `{type, text}` object, so even a literal `<script>` typed by a user can only ever become an inert ProseMirror text node (auto-escaped by the DOM), never live markup. TipTap's own schema (only the configured node/mark types exist at all) additionally means pasted HTML outside that set is dropped by ProseMirror's parser, not executed.
- Photo tokens resolve only against photoIds the report itself already owns (never a client-supplied URL) — this is itself the sanitization for the image-insert feature; there is no arbitrary-image-loading/SSRF/src-injection surface at all.
- New test coverage: `backend/test/rich-content.test.js` (13 tests — tokenizer, escaping, sanitizer bounds, hostile-content-survives-unstripped) and `backend/test/rich-export.test.js` (8 tests — hostile caption text in HTML export, preamble/photo/table/list/page-break correctness across all 3 export formats).

**A real bug found and fixed during live testing (Phase 9's own "fix every bug found" requirement):** `backend/utils/documentGenerator.js`'s local `parseReportSections()` (a second, simpler section-splitter used only for DOCX, distinct from the frontend's) silently dropped any content appearing **before the first `## ` heading** — `current` stayed `null` until the first heading line, and the `else if (current)` branch only appended when `current` was already truthy. This was a low-stakes, pre-existing gap before Phase 9 (typically just the report's `# INSURANCE INSPECTION REPORT` H1 line), but Phase 9's Regenerate Section/AI functions can legitimately place real content — a live-tested example put a new "Inspection Overview" narrative, a single photo, and a 2-photo grid there — into exactly that pre-first-`##`-heading position (the frontend's own "Introduction" pseudo-section, which always re-serializes with no `##` prefix — see `frontend/src/utils/reportSections.js`). Confirmed via direct export against the real generated report: the DOCX export showed 0 `<w:drawing>` elements and no page break despite `photoMap` containing the right entries, while the PDF and HTML exports (which never pre-split into sections) rendered everything correctly. **Fixed** by having `parseReportSections()` collect that leading content into a `{title: null, ...}` pseudo-section and having the section-XML builder skip emitting a synthetic Word heading for a `null` title (rendering just the content, matching PDF/HTML's existing behavior). Re-verified against the same real report: DOCX now shows the correct `<w:drawing>` count and preamble text. Locked in with 2 new regression tests in `rich-export.test.js` (`'DOCX: content before the first ## heading is no longer silently dropped'`, plus the photo/grid-embedding test using that same preamble-positioned content).

**Files created:** `backend/utils/richContent.js`, `backend/test/rich-content.test.js`, `backend/test/rich-export.test.js`, `backend/test/section-assist.test.js`, `frontend/src/utils/richContent.js`, `frontend/src/__tests__/richContent.test.js`, `frontend/src/components/reportEditor/extensions.js` (PageBreak/ReportPhoto/ReportPhotoGrid TipTap nodes).
**Files rewritten:** `frontend/src/components/SectionedReportEditor.jsx` (plain-textarea → full TipTap editor, section management, AI panel, Regenerate Section modal — same external props as before).
**Files modified:** `backend/services/aiService.js` (section title renames; `buildSectionSuggestionPrompt`/`suggestReportSection` extended with `instructions`; new `assistReportSection`/`buildAssistPrompt`/`SECTION_ASSIST_ACTIONS`), `backend/routes/reports.js` (sanitize on `PUT`/`approve`; new `POST /:id/sections/assist`; `/sections/suggest` accepts `instructions`; export route resolves referenced photoIds into a `photoMap` and passes it to all 3 generators), `backend/utils/properPdfGenerator.js` (real inline bold/italic/underline via PDFKit's native `continued`/`underline` text options instead of stripping; numbered lists; `{{page-break}}` → real `addPage()`; single/grid photo rendering with placeholder fallback; ToC + field-list-section regex updated for new titles), `backend/utils/documentGenerator.js` (same inline-run/numbered-list/page-break/photo handling for OOXML — including genuine `<w:drawing>` image embedding: media parts, `Content_Types`/relationship entries, EMU-sized drawings via real `sharp`-read dimensions; the preamble-drop bug fix), `backend/routes/reports.js`'s `generateHTML` (rewritten from a single global regex-replace into a real line-by-line block renderer — semantic `<table>`/`<ul>`/`<ol>`, embedded photos as base64 data URIs, a `.page-break` divider — while keeping the exact same hostile-content-escaping guarantee the existing test already checks), `frontend/src/services/api.js` (`assistSection` added), `frontend/src/index.css` (editor/page-break/photo-node/photo-grid styling), `frontend/package.json`/`package-lock.json` (9 new `@tiptap/*` dependencies).

**Testing/verification performed:**
- **Automated:** backend `npm test` → **135/135 passing** (29 new: 13 `rich-content.test.js` + 8 `rich-export.test.js` + 8 `section-assist.test.js`), `npm run lint` → **0 errors, 43 warnings** (unchanged documented baseline). Frontend `npm test` → **15/15 passing** (11 new `richContent.test.js` round-trip tests), `npm run lint` → **0 errors, 15 warnings** (unchanged baseline), `npm run build` → succeeds (the `SectionedReportEditor` chunk is now ~390KB/~117KB gzipped, flagged by Vite's own >500KB warning at the `index` chunk level — a real, accepted bundle-size cost of TipTap, not a regression needing a fix in this phase).
- **Live, end-to-end, against the real Anthropic API and a real Firebase project** (a throwaway QA account created via the Firebase Admin SDK, `emailVerified: true`, no real inbox needed; a second throwaway account for the cross-user authorization check; both fully deleted afterward along with their reports, Storage objects, and version subcollections — confirmed via `git status`, zero residue in the repo):
  - **Full wizard-to-generate pass** (Water Damage demo template, 3 real generated JPEGs uploaded) — real report generated against the live Anthropic API; confirmed the new `SECTION 1: REPORT INFO`/`SECTION 2: CLAIM INFO & INSURED INFO` titles appear correctly in the actual generated content, not just in the prompt source.
  - **Formatting controls** (9/9 checks): Bold/Italic/Underline toolbar buttons apply real `<strong>`/`<em>`/`<u>` marks; numbered list creates a real `<ol>` (verified clean in the realistic type-then-convert-to-list flow — an earlier automated test using an unusual convert-then-Enter-then-toggle keyboard sequence produced a merged-text artifact that was root-caused as Playwright-automation-speed noise, not a product bug, and does not reproduce in normal usage, confirmed by 2 follow-up isolated tests); table insert, page break insert all create the correct nodes; Undo/Redo confirmed correct (type → undo reverts → redo re-applies).
  - **Section management** (5/5 checks): Add Section, Rename, Move Up (reorder), and Delete Section (with confirm) all confirmed working with no data loss, including finding a renamed section correctly after a reorder.
  - **Photo insert** (single + grid, 4/4 checks): the photo picker lists the report's real uploaded photos as authenticated-blob thumbnails; inserting one creates a `reportPhoto` node; inserting 2 creates a `reportPhotoGrid` node with exactly 2 children. Visually confirmed via screenshot (thumbnails, captions, remove buttons all render correctly).
  - **AI panel** (Improve Writing, Expand, Review Photo Documentation all live-tested against the real API; the other 4 covered by the 8 `section-assist.test.js` prompt-construction tests plus sharing the identical code path): Discard leaves content unchanged; Apply changes it; the Apply/Discard panel renders correctly for both a plain rewrite (Expand) and a photo-context-aware function (Review Photo Documentation).
  - **Regenerate Section** (4/4 checks): opens the instructions modal; Cancel discards with no change; Regenerate shows a real Current-vs-Generated comparison from the live API; Approve & Replace changes the section content.
  - **Save/persist round-trip:** after Save Changes, direct Firestore inspection confirmed the exact new dialect tokens persisted correctly — `![[photo:ID|caption]]`, the `[[photos cols=2]]...[[/photos]]` block, and `**bold**` markdown all present verbatim in the saved `content` string.
  - **Approve & Finalize:** full signature form (name/title/license number/license state/company + confirmation checkbox) submitted successfully; report status became `finalized`; Firestore `signature` object confirmed correct; the finalized PDF/HTML exports confirmed to have **no** DRAFT watermark and to include the real signature name/license text.
  - **Export compatibility (PDF/DOCX/HTML), verified via a direct authenticated API call** (bypassing the browser's download-event handling, which showed file-ordering artifacts under 3 rapid sequential clicks — confirmed to be a Playwright/Chromium download-event-sequencing artifact, not a backend bug, by this direct-API check returning byte-perfect, correctly-typed files every time): PDF (6 pages, includes the page break), DOCX (2 real embedded JPEG media parts, 3 `<w:drawing>` elements, real `<w:br w:type="page"/>`, real per-run `<w:b/>` bold — all XML confirmed well-formed via `@xmldom/xmldom` with zero parse errors), HTML (3 `data:image/jpeg;base64,...` embedded photos, a `.photo-grid` div, a `.page-break` div).
  - **Authorization/sanitization (7/7 direct HTTP checks):** unauthenticated `GET /:id/photos` → 401; a genuinely different second account → 404 (not leaked) on both `GET /:id` and the new `/sections/assist`/`/sections/suggest` endpoints; an invalid `action` value → 400; a 400,000-character `PUT /:id` content payload → accepted (200, reopens as draft) with the persisted content correctly capped at exactly 300,000 characters.
  - **Legacy-report compatibility:** a genuinely synthesized pre-Phase-6/8/9-shaped report (plain `content` string, no `photos` array, no `review` state) opened in the new rich editor with zero crashes and zero console errors, correctly rendering as 3 editable sections (the `#` H1 title line is itself a section under the existing, unchanged `parseReportSections` heading-level rule, plus the report's 2 `##` sections).
  - **Mobile viewport (390×844):** zero horizontal overflow; the toolbar wraps cleanly into two rows; all section cards, the AI panel, and the Review & Approval panel render correctly; zero console errors.
  - Zero console errors and zero failed/5xx HTTP requests were observed across every one of the above passes (the one 500-adjacent event during testing — a `POST /approve` that appeared to hang in one run — was traced to this session's own `nodemon` restarting mid-request because a `prettier --write` was run concurrently against a watched file; the approval had actually already succeeded server-side by the time the response was cut, confirmed via direct Firestore read; not a product bug).

**Not covered live (flagged, not glossed over):** Check Consistency/Check Missing Information/Rewrite Professionally/Shorten were not individually driven through a live browser click (Improve Writing, Expand, and Review Photo Documentation were, covering the "plain rewrite" and "context-aware" code shapes) — correctness for the remaining 4 rests on the 8 `section-assist.test.js` prompt-construction tests (every action's prompt built with the correct inputs) plus the fact all 7 share one execution path (`assistReportSection`/`buildAssistPrompt`) already exercised live. True concurrent-editor races on the same section were not tested (an unlikely scenario for a single-adjuster workflow, same reasoning as Phase 8). Drag-and-drop section reordering was not built (Move Up/Down arrows were used instead — explicitly an allowed alternative per this task's own wording, chosen for reliability/accessibility).

**Risks/considerations, carried forward:** Phase 30 (routing migration) is still not done — the rich editor remains reachable only via the existing `Dashboard.jsx activeView === 'generate'` view (from a fresh generation, or via My Reports' "Review & edit" shield-check action, which was verified this session to correctly re-enter the editor for an already-generated report), not a real `/reports/:id/edit` URL. This phase did not need Phase 30 because it upgraded the *existing* editor component in place rather than adding a new page/route — but Phase 13 (Real Template Builder), which explicitly depends on Phase 9's section model, will still need Phase 30 for its own new `/templates`/`/templates/new` pages. The TipTap dependency (~9 packages, ~117KB gzipped for the editor chunk) is a one-time bundle-size cost accepted as part of this phase's own "OPEN QUESTION" resolution — no further action needed unless the client wants code-splitting/lazy-loading investigated later (Phase 27's performance-completion territory, not this phase's).

## Phase 10 — Report Review Checklist & Approval Modal

**Objective:** Build the spec'd pre-approval review checklist (with warnings) and add a confirmation modal to the existing approval action.

**Reason this phase is required:** Item 28 (`PARTIALLY COMPLETE`) — no checklist (Claim Data/Inspection Data/Photos N-of-N/Documentation/Draft Sections), no yellow warnings, and approval fires immediately with no confirmation step.

**Scope:** Frontend review screen + minor backend read-endpoint to compute checklist completeness.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Build a review checklist component checking: claim number/insured/loss date present; inspection info complete (needs Phase 5); photos N/N analyzed-and-reviewed (needs Phase 8); documentation processed (needs Phase 5); draft sections completion.
2. Add yellow warning banners for: missing claim number, excluded photos, unreviewed photos, missing inspection date — using only neutral, non-settlement language (already confirmed compliant elsewhere in the app).
3. Add a confirmation modal ("Approve this report?" + explanatory text + Cancel/Approve) before the existing `POST /:id/approve` call fires.

**Files/components likely affected:** `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (review/approval view), `backend/routes/reports.js` (a small summary/checklist-status read, if not composable client-side from existing data).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Phase 5 (documentation/inspection fields must exist to check them) and Phase 8 (per-photo review status must exist to check "N/N reviewed").

**Expected output:** A real pre-approval checklist gate with a deliberate confirmation step.

**Acceptance criteria:** Warnings appear correctly for incomplete reports; approval requires an explicit modal confirmation; approving still records the same signature/audit data as today (no regression).

**Testing/verification steps:** Attempt approval on a report missing claim number/with excluded photos — confirm warnings show; confirm modal appears and Cancel aborts approval; confirm Approve still completes the existing backend flow unchanged.

**Risks/considerations:** Keep this additive to the existing, already-solid approval backend — do not rework `POST /:id/approve` itself in this phase.

**Status:** ✅ **Completed (2026-08-18)**, fully live-verified end-to-end against the real Anthropic API and real Firebase project (desktop + mobile, complete + incomplete reports), including both `Dashboard.jsx` and `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`.

**Implementation summary:**
- New `frontend/src/components/ReportReviewChecklist.jsx` — a pure, read-only summary component computing 5 checklist rows entirely from data the app already has: **Claim Data** (claimNumber/insuredName/propertyAddress/lossDate all present), **Inspection Data** (inspectionDate + inspectorName present — both optional Phase 5 fields), **Photos** (analyzed vs. reviewed counts, from each photo's real `analysisStatus`/`review.status`), **Documentation** (`report.documents.length`), **Draft Sections** (a real count of `## ` headings in `report.content`, not a fabricated percentage). Three yellow warning banners render only when genuinely true: missing claim number, excluded and/or unreviewed photos, missing inspection date — worded neutrally, no settlement/verdict language (Golden Rule #2). No completion count or percentage is ever synthesized; a report with 0 photos or 0 documents shows an honest "None" state, not a hidden or invented number.
- **Live per-photo data problem, solved without a new backend endpoint:** Phase 8's interactive Photo Review gallery (`ReportPhotoGallery` in `Dashboard.jsx`) keeps its own local per-photo review state that changes as the reviewer Approves/Excludes/Edits — the report doc's own `photos` snapshot (returned once at generation time) goes stale the moment any review action happens. Rather than add a backend read-endpoint (the phase's own "if not composable client-side" escape hatch applied — it was composable), `ReportPhotoGallery` gained an optional `onPhotosChange` prop, fired with the live array on both its initial `GET /:id/photos` fetch and every successful review action; `Dashboard.jsx` threads this into a new `reviewPhotos` state passed straight into the checklist, so it reflects Approve/Exclude/Edit actions immediately with zero extra network calls. `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` has no interactive gallery of its own (a pre-existing, separate gap — see below), so there the checklist component fetches `GET /:id/photos` itself when no `photos` prop is supplied — the same component works standalone or wired-live depending on the caller.
- **Confirmation modal:** reused the existing `ConfirmDialog` component (already used elsewhere for delete/revoke confirmations) rather than building a new one. The "Approve & Finalize" button's `onClick` was split into `handleApproveClick` (keeps the existing client-side signature/confirm-checkbox validation, then opens the modal) and `handleApprove` (the actual `POST /:id/approve` call, now fired only from the modal's own Approve button). Modal message states in plain language what approving will do (finalize, remove the DRAFT watermark, require re-approval after any future edit) and names the reviewer being attested. Cancel closes the modal with zero side effects; approving still runs the exact same, unmodified backend call (signature/audit log/version/webhook all untouched, per the phase's explicit scope guardrail against reworking `POST /:id/approve`). Implemented identically in both `Dashboard.jsx` and `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`.
- **A real bug found and fixed during live testing:** wiring `onPhotosChange` into `ReportPhotoGallery`'s `doReview` initially called it from inside the `setPhotos(prev => ...)` updater function — this fires during React's render-phase state resolution and calling a *different* component's setter (`Dashboard`'s `setReviewPhotos`) from there triggered a genuine "Cannot update a component while rendering a different component" React warning on every photo review action. Fixed by computing the next photos array from the `photos` closure variable directly (this handler is only ever invoked from a single user click, so no staleness risk) and calling `setPhotos`/`onPhotosChange` as two independent, top-level calls instead of nesting one inside the other's updater.
- **A pre-existing, unrelated gap found (not fixed, out of Phase 10's scope):** `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s `handleGenerate()` still assumes Phase 7's synchronous pre-pipeline model — it treats the immediate `POST /generate` response as the finished report and calls `autoPreviewPdf()` right away, even though the backend now responds instantly with `status: 'processing'`/`content: null` and finishes the real work in the background. This dashboard never got Phase 7's polling/analysis-view treatment that `Dashboard.jsx` has. The new checklist handles this gracefully — it shows an honest "no content yet" / zero-photos-analyzed state rather than crashing or fabricating data — but the underlying dashboard would benefit from the same async-aware treatment `Dashboard.jsx` already has. Flagged for a future session, not actioned here.
- No backend changes were needed — every field the checklist reads already existed (Phase 5's claim/inspection fields, Phase 6/8's per-photo `analysisStatus`/`review`, Phase 5's `documents` array, the existing Markdown `content`).

**Files touched:** `frontend/src/components/ReportReviewChecklist.jsx` (new), `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (checklist card, confirmation modal, `onPhotosChange` wiring, the setState-during-render fix), `frontend/src/pages/EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` (checklist card, confirmation modal, mirroring `Dashboard.jsx`).

**Testing/verification — automated:** Backend `npm test` → **135/135 passing** (unchanged — no backend code touched), `npm run lint` → 0 errors/43 warnings (unchanged baseline). Frontend `npm run lint` → 0 errors/15 warnings (unchanged baseline, briefly 16 before the setState-during-render fix's `eslint-disable-next-line` was added to match the file's existing pattern for that effect), `npm test` → **15/15 passing** (unchanged), `npm run build` → succeeds.

**Testing/verification — live** (3 throwaway QA accounts created via the Firebase Admin SDK — 2 agency-tier with different data-completeness profiles, 1 enterprise-tier — all fully deleted afterward along with their reports, Storage objects, and Firestore docs; confirmed zero repo residue via `git status`):
- **Desktop, a fully-complete report** (all claim/policy/insurance/inspection fields filled, 2 photos, 1 document, generated against the real Anthropic API): checklist correctly showed all 5 rows accurate before any photo review (Claim Data/Inspection Data/Documentation/Draft Sections complete, Photos "0 of 2 reviewed" with a matching warning); after approving both photos in the gallery, the checklist updated live to "2 of 2 reviewed" with zero warnings — no page reload needed. The confirmation modal was exercised both ways: Cancel left the report as an unreviewed draft; a second click through the modal's own Approve button correctly finalized the report (signature persisted, DRAFT watermark condition cleared), which then survived a full page refresh. **16/17 automated checks passed** — the one failure was a local dev rate-limiter (10 req/60s on `/generate`) tripped by this session's own rapid repeated test runs, not a defect; a backend restart (which resets the in-memory limiter) and re-run confirmed the underlying flow was correct all along.
- **Desktop, a deliberately incomplete report** (inspection date/inspector left blank, one photo excluded, one left unreviewed): checklist correctly showed "Inspection Data" incomplete and simultaneously surfaced all three warning types together — missing inspection date, one photo excluded, one photo still unreviewed — with accurate counts throughout. The confirmation modal's Cancel path was re-verified here too. **9/9 core checks passed** (a same-session 429 from cumulative test traffic was resolved by a backend restart and confirmed as a testing artifact, not a regression).
- **Mobile (390×844):** reopened an existing draft report via the report list's "Review and edit report" action (the row itself opens a separate read-only summary modal without the checklist/approval flow — a pre-existing, intentional distinction in this codebase, not a Phase 10 gap) — confirmed zero horizontal overflow, the checklist rendered correctly, and the confirmation modal opened and could be filled/cancelled on a real mobile viewport. **3/4 core checks passed** (the 4th was again a rate-limiter artifact from cumulative session traffic, cleared by a restart).
- **EnterpriseDashboard spot check:** confirmed the checklist reaches this dashboard too, self-fetching its own photo data (no interactive gallery exists there to feed it live), and — critically — does **not** crash or fabricate data when handed a still-`processing`/null-content report (the pre-existing Phase 7 gap noted above). **4/4 checks passed.**
- Zero console errors and zero unexpected failed/5xx HTTP requests across every pass, aside from the explicitly-identified, self-inflicted rate-limiter artifacts from this session's own back-to-back automated test runs (each confirmed transient via a backend restart + clean re-run, not a product defect).

**Not covered live:** a literal concurrent-editor race on the checklist/photo-review data (same reasoning as Phases 7-9 — an unlikely scenario for a single-adjuster workflow); a real end-to-end approval completed specifically through `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` (the spot check confirmed the checklist itself works there, but a full approve-and-finalize cycle through that dashboard was not separately exercised, since it shares the identical `handleApprove`/modal code already proven in `Dashboard.jsx`).

## Phase 11 — Export Options Modal & PDF Layout Completion

**Objective:** Add a real export options modal and complete the PDF layout gaps (confidentiality statement, configurable photo-per-page layout).

**Reason this phase is required:** Item 29 (`PARTIALLY COMPLETE`) — export is a single button per format with no options; Item 49 notes the PDF generator has no confidentiality-statement text and a hardcoded 2-column photo layout.

**Scope:** Frontend export modal, backend export endpoint options, PDF generator template updates.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Build an export options modal with checkboxes: Include cover page, Include photo captions, Include page numbers, Include appendix, Include company branding.
2. Extend `POST /:id/export` to accept these options (currently only `format` + `includeImages`) and thread them through to the generators.
3. Add a confidentiality-statement line to the PDF/DOCX footer (`properPdfGenerator.js`, `documentGenerator.js`).
4. Ensure claim # appears in the footer (currently header-only).
5. Add configurable photo-documentation layout: 1/2/4 photos per page, each with photo number/caption/area/observation (area/observation require Phase 8's per-photo structured data).

**Files/components likely affected:** `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (export UI), `backend/routes/reports.js` (`POST /:id/export`), `backend/utils/properPdfGenerator.js`, `backend/utils/documentGenerator.js`.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Photo area/observation-per-image layout option benefits from Phase 8; cover-page/captions/page-number/branding options can be done independently sooner if desired.

**Expected output:** Users can configure what appears in their exported report.

**Acceptance criteria:** Each checkbox measurably changes the exported file; confidentiality statement and claim # appear in the footer of every export; photo layout options render correctly at 1/2/4 per page.

**Testing/verification steps:** Export with every checkbox combination and visually confirm each option's effect in the resulting PDF/DOCX.

**Risks/considerations:** Keep the default behavior (no options selected) backward-compatible with current export output where reasonable.

**Amendment (2026-08-16 full-spec audit) — renamed scope + 1 additional task:**
- **Phase title/scope expanded to include a Report Preview page.** Verified: no `/reports/:id/preview` route or page exists anywhere; PDF preview today is an inline `<iframe>` auto-triggered within the existing generate/editor view (`Dashboard.jsx`'s `autoPreviewPDF`), not its own dedicated page with a Desktop/PDF toggle.
6. **Build a full report-preview page** (spec Page 14) with a Desktop-rendering / PDF-rendering toggle and Edit/Approve/Export actions, reusing this phase's export-options work for the PDF side and the editor's rendered content for the Desktop side. (See the routing-architecture Open Question — whether this is a real `/reports/:id/preview` URL or another `Dashboard.jsx` `activeView` depends on that decision.)

**Routing-architecture decision made this session (2026-08-18):** built as a real, standalone `/reports/:id/preview` route (`ProtectedRoute` + `ReportPreviewPage.jsx`), added to `App.jsx` ahead of the full Phase 30 migration — same precedent as Phase 9 (rich editor) and Phase 10 (checklist), which also shipped inside the existing `activeView` architecture without needing Phase 30 first. The page fetches its report independently by ID (`reportsAPI.getOne`), so it works on a direct URL visit or a refresh with no dependency on `Dashboard.jsx`'s in-memory state. Its "Edit" action deep-links back to `Dashboard.jsx` as `/dashboard?openReport=<id>` (a new, narrowly-scoped query-param handler added to `Dashboard.jsx`, not a routing migration) which opens the same report in the existing generate/review view.

## ✅ Completed (2026-08-18)

**Implementation summary:**
- **`ExportOptionsModal.jsx` (new, shared)** — format picker (PDF/DOCX/HTML, locked per `tier.exportFormats`), the 5 required checkboxes (cover page, photo captions, page numbers, photo appendix, company branding — all default ON, matching pre-Phase-11 output for backward compatibility), and a 1/2/4 photos-per-page layout picker (shown only when the report has photos and the appendix is enabled). Used by both `Dashboard.jsx` (replacing the old fixed-format quick-download-only card, which is kept alongside as a "Customize…" link for one-click default exports) and the new preview page.
- **`ReportPreviewPage.jsx` (new)** — real `/reports/:id/preview` route. Desktop mode renders `report.content` through the existing `ReportMarkdown` component; PDF mode lazy-loads an inline `<iframe>` preview (same export→download→blob-URL pattern as `Dashboard.jsx`'s existing PDF preview). Edit/Approve/Export controls are gated on report status (`processing`/`regenerating` disables Approve and Export with a locked, titled button instead of hiding them outright); Approve opens a self-contained signature+confirm modal that calls the existing, unchanged `POST /:id/approve`. Loading and error states (not-found vs. generic failure, with Retry) are explicit, not blank screens.
- **`POST /:id/export` (backend)** — now accepts and server-side validates 5 boolean options (non-boolean/missing → default `true`, so an old client gets unchanged behavior) plus `photoLayout` (clamped to `{1,2,4}`, default `2`). A new `buildAppendixPhotoList()` helper resolves the report's real, reviewer-approved photo data (Phase 8's `review.status !== 'excluded'`, `review.status === 'edited' ? review.observation : analysis.observation`) into the appendix — never fabricated, and legacy pre-Phase-6 reports (no `photos` array) fall back to the flat `imagePaths` list with no invented location/observation.
- **Real bug found and fixed — Golden Rule #4 gap:** `tier.exportFormats` existed in `backend/config/tiers.js` but was **never checked** in `POST /:id/export` before this phase. A starter-tier account could already get a clean DOCX/HTML export by calling the API directly (bypassing the UI, which only offered PDF for that tier) — pure client-side gating, not enforced server-side. Fixed: the route now returns `403 EXPORT_FORMAT_NOT_ALLOWED` for a disallowed format. Verified live (see Testing below).
- **`properPdfGenerator.js`** — cover page is now optional (`includeCoverPage`); when off, the Table of Contents becomes page 1 and the post-process header/footer pass no longer exempts it. `includeCompanyBranding` toggles the cover's logo/brand label and the per-page header bar's logo/text (not the small footer attribution line, which stays visible in every format — see the cross-format consistency fix below). The old fixed 2-column "PHOTO APPENDIX" block was replaced with a real 1/2/4-column layout: each cell measures its own caption/area/observation text height first (`doc.heightOfString`, no side effects) so rows page-break cleanly instead of clipping. Footer now always carries `Claim {number} — {confidentiality statement}` on its own line, with the existing "Page N" field now independently toggleable via `includePageNumbers`.
- **`documentGenerator.js`** — mirrors the PDF's option set: the cover block (title/watermark/"Prepared by"/details table) is now a single conditional `coverXml` block; a new `resolveAppendixAssets()`/`buildAppendixXml()` pair builds a real bordered table (1/2/4 cols) with an embedded `<w:drawing>` image plus number/caption/area/observation paragraphs per cell, registered into the same Content-Types/rels/media wiring as the existing inline-photo-token assets (distinct `rIdAppendix*` relationship ids to avoid collisions). Footer gained a second paragraph with the confidentiality statement + claim number.
- **`generateHTML` (`backend/routes/reports.js`)** — same option set; `includeCoverPage=false` drops the colored `.header` block down to a plain `<h1>`; a new `buildAppendixHtml()` renders the appendix as a CSS-grid of `<figure>` cards with inline `data:` URIs (no separate authenticated image endpoint needed for a static HTML file); footer gained the confidentiality/claim line.
- **Cross-format consistency fix found during visual verification:** initially, unchecking "Include company branding" hid the small footer brand-attribution line in DOCX and HTML but *not* in PDF (whose footer text was never gated by that option). Standardized on the PDF's original behavior across all three generators — `includeCompanyBranding` toggles the cover mark + header-bar logo/text only; the footer's brand attribution (paired with the always-on confidentiality line) is never hidden by this option in any format, since it is closer to a generation-attribution/legal footer than "your brand."

**Files touched:** `backend/routes/reports.js` (export route options/entitlement check/`buildAppendixPhotoList`/`generateHTML`/`buildAppendixHtml`), `backend/utils/properPdfGenerator.js`, `backend/utils/documentGenerator.js`, `frontend/src/App.jsx` (new route), `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (export modal wiring, `?openReport=` deep link, "Open Full Preview" entry points in the generate view / My Reports row / `ReportDetailModal`). **New:** `frontend/src/components/ExportOptionsModal.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/ReportPreviewPage.jsx`. `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` was **not** touched (out of this phase's stated file list; its report data model is compatible with the new export options with no changes needed).

**Testing/verification — automated:**
- Backend: 135/135 tests pass (11 export-specific), 0 lint errors, 43 warnings (pre-existing baseline, unchanged).
- Frontend: 15/15 tests pass, 0 lint errors, 15 warnings (pre-existing baseline, unchanged), production build succeeds.
- A direct unit smoke test (`generatePDF`/`generateDOCX` called with 3, then 9, synthetic photos across all three layouts) confirmed: default options reproduce the pre-Phase-11 page count exactly (backward compatibility); every combination of toggles renders without throwing; page count scales sensibly with photo count and layout density (9 photos → 9/7/6 pages at layout 1/2/4).

**Testing/verification — live (real dev servers, real Anthropic API, real Firebase project, Playwright/Chromium, 2 throwaway accounts — professional and starter tier — both fully deleted after use):**
- Generated a real report (2 photos, wind/hail claim) as the professional-tier account, approved both photos in the gallery so the appendix would have real reviewer-approved observations, then opened it via the new "Open Full Preview" button — confirmed it navigates to a real `/reports/:id/preview` URL.
- On the preview page: direct URL load ✅, Desktop mode renders the markdown content ✅, PDF mode renders a live iframe preview ✅, toggling back to Desktop ✅.
- Exported real files through the modal: **PDF** (cover off, 4-per-page layout) — 35.8KB, downloaded and byte-verified; **DOCX** (branding off) — 12.1KB; **HTML** (all defaults) — 41.6KB. All three downloads were driven end-to-end through the real backend/Storage/Anthropic-generated content, not mocked.
- **Real text extracted from the downloaded PDFs** (via `pdfjs-dist`, rendered locally) confirmed, across default and minimal-option combinations: cover page present/absent correctly; "FLACRONAI" cover label and per-page header logo/text present/absent correctly with `includeCompanyBranding`; photo captions (filenames) present/absent correctly with `includePhotoCaptions`; the Photo Documentation appendix shows real per-photo `Area: …` and cautious AI-generated observation text (e.g. "This image appears to be a uniform solid red-orange field... No assessable condition or damage appears present in this photo."); the confidentiality statement + claim number appear in the footer on **every** export regardless of options; "Page N" is present/absent correctly with `includePageNumbers`; report body language throughout stayed cautious ("does not constitute a final determination of cause, coverage, liability, or loss value", "Adjuster to confirm...") — Golden Rule #2 intact.
- **Real DOCX XML inspected directly** (via `pizzip`) for the same option combinations: embedded appendix images present (2 media parts), PHOTO DOCUMENTATION table present, cover Title-style paragraph and "Prepared by" line present/absent correctly, footer brand/confidentiality/page-field all correct — this is what surfaced the cross-format branding inconsistency (see above), fixed and re-verified in place.
- Approve modal on the preview page: filled signature fields, confirmed, approved — report became Finalized; **refresh persistence** confirmed (Finalized status and hidden Approve button survive a full page reload); **Edit** deep-link confirmed to reopen the *same* report in `Dashboard.jsx`'s review view.
- **Authorization:** logged in as the separate starter-tier account and navigated directly to the professional account's report's preview URL — got a clean "Report not found" state with zero claim data leaked (no info-disclosure via a differently-worded error).
- **Entitlement enforcement (server-side, via a captured real bearer token, not a UI click):** starter tier calling `POST /:id/export` directly with `format: 'docx'` or `'html'` → `403 EXPORT_FORMAT_NOT_ALLOWED`; the same call with `format: 'pdf'` → `200`. This is the live confirmation of the Golden Rule #4 fix above.

## Phase 12 — My Reports & Claims Management Completion

**Objective:** Complete the missing filters and row actions on My Reports and Claims, and add a Photos section to the claim workspace.

**Reason this phase is required:** Item 30 (`PARTIALLY COMPLETE`) — no type/creator/date/org filters despite backend support; no Duplicate action anywhere; Archive exists server-side but is unreachable from the UI; Claims list has no search/date filters and no Edit/Duplicate/Archive/Delete actions despite backend endpoints existing unused; claim workspace has no Photos tab.

**Scope:** Frontend list/table UI for both My Reports and Claims; one new backend endpoint (Duplicate) if it doesn't exist for reports.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Expose the existing backend filter params (type/creator/date/org) as UI controls on My Reports.
2. Wire the My Reports "Delete" action to offer both Archive (soft, existing backend support) and permanent Delete as distinct choices, rather than only hard-deleting.
3. Add a Duplicate action for reports (new backend endpoint if none exists — copy claim data into a new draft, do not duplicate photos/analysis).
4. Add search + date filters to the Claims list; wire the already-existing `PUT`/`DELETE /claims/:id` backend endpoints to real Edit/Archive/Delete UI actions.
5. Add a Photos tab/section to `CRMClaimProfile.jsx` (aggregating photos across the claim's linked reports).

**Files/components likely affected:** `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (reports view), `frontend/src/pages/CRM.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/CRMClaimProfile.jsx`, `backend/routes/reports.js` (duplicate endpoint), `backend/routes/crm.js`.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Soft dependency on Phase 5 — duplicating a report should carry over the fuller field set once it exists, to avoid duplicating twice.

**Expected output:** My Reports and Claims become fully manageable list views matching spec actions.

**Acceptance criteria:** All listed filters and actions function and are backed by real data; Duplicate produces a correct new draft; Archive and Delete are clearly distinct actions.

**Testing/verification steps:** Exercise every filter and every row action on both lists against seeded test data; confirm archived reports don't appear in default views but remain retrievable.

**Risks/considerations:** Decide whether "Duplicate" should also duplicate uploaded photos (storage cost) or start the new report with a clean photo set — recommend clean/no-photos duplicate, flag as open question if unclear.

**Amendment (2026-08-16 full-spec audit):** the spec's nav (§9) and URL structure (§36) list "Claims" as a top-level item available to everyone, but the current `/claims` functionality lives entirely inside `CRM.jsx`, tier-gated to Agency/Enterprise. **Added as an Open Question below** — should claim-centric organization be available to all tiers (Starter/Professional included), or is CRM/Claims intentionally an Agency+ upsell? This changes this phase's scope meaningfully (a Starter-tier "Claims" view with no CRM would need its own simpler design).

**Implementation decision on the above open question (2026-08-18):** this amendment's own text says the question was "added as an Open Question below," but a re-check of the "Open Questions Requiring Client/Team Decision" section found it was never actually enumerated there (a documentation gap, similar to others already noted elsewhere in this file) — it remains genuinely unresolved. Per the client's explicit instruction this session ("follow the documented Claims tier-gating decision; if still unresolved, stop and ask before implementing that specific behavior"), this phase deliberately did **not** touch tier-gating: Claims/CRM remains exactly as tier-gated as it already was (Agency/Enterprise only, `requireTier('agency')` server-side + a matching frontend upsell gate) — every task below only completes the *existing* Agency+ Claims feature's filters/actions, none of which requires deciding whether to expose it more broadly. The broader "should Starter/Professional get a simpler Claims view" question remains open and is flagged again below for a future decision, not answered here.

## ✅ Completed (2026-08-18)

**Implementation summary:**
- **My Reports filters (`Dashboard.jsx`)** — a collapsible "Filters" panel (default closed, so the existing simple search+status bar is unchanged until opened) adds: Claim # (dedicated exact-ish substring match, separate from the general search box), Loss Type, Report Type, Creator (email substring), Created-date range, and Organization (a live CRM-client dropdown, shown only for Agency/Enterprise tiers that actually have CRM clients). All six are real, server-side filters — `GET /:id`'s list route (`backend/routes/reports.js`) gained `reportType`, `clientId`, `claimNumber`, and `creator` query params alongside the pre-existing `lossType`/`status`/`startDate`/`endDate`/`search`. **Creator is real but currently single-valued per account** — reports have no shared team-visibility pool yet (Phase 14 territory), so a new `createdByEmail` field is stored at generate time and is genuinely filterable, but every report in one account's own list necessarily shares the same creator today; this is honest, forward-compatible plumbing, not a fabricated feature.
- **My Reports row actions** — Open/View, Edit ("Review & edit"), and Full Preview stayed as direct icon buttons (unchanged, lowest regression risk); a new "More actions" kebab menu (`RowActionsMenu`, a new local component in `Dashboard.jsx`) adds Duplicate, Download PDF, Share (disabled with a tooltip unless the report is finalized), and Archive-or-Restore (whichever applies), with permanent Delete moved into the same menu. Bulk actions gained "Archive Selected" alongside the existing "Delete Selected."
- **Archive vs. permanent Delete, clearly separated** — Archive now has its own confirm dialog (non-danger style, "Archive this report? ... Nothing is deleted") distinct from permanent Delete's existing danger-styled dialog ("This permanently deletes... cannot be undone"). Archiving now also stores `preArchiveStatus` on the report so a new `POST /:id/restore` endpoint can put a restored report back to exactly the status it had before (draft or finalized) rather than always resetting to draft — protecting a finalized report's clean-export state (Golden Rule #3) across an archive/restore round trip.
- **Duplicate (new `POST /:id/duplicate`)** — copies the complete Phase 5 claim/inspection field set (all 19 fields plus the core claim/loss fields and `clientId`/`claimId`) into a brand-new draft; deliberately does **not** copy photos, supporting documents, AI-generated content, or review/approval state, per this phase's own risk note's recommendation (clean/no-photos duplicate). The new claim number gets a visible " (Copy)" suffix so the duplicate is never confused with the original in the list.
- **Claims list (`CRM.jsx`)** — added a real search box (claim #/loss type/address) and a loss-date range, both applied server-side via `crmService.getClaims`'s extended `startDate`/`endDate`/`archived` params (previously client-side status-only filtering over an unfiltered fetch). Added Edit (reusing `NewClaimModal` in a new edit mode — pre-fills the form and calls the already-existing `PUT /claims/:id` instead of `POST /claims`), Archive/Restore (a new `archived` boolean field, toggled via the same existing `PUT /claims/:id` — no new backend route needed, validated server-side via a new `express-validator` rule), and Delete (the existing, unmodified `DELETE /claims/:id`) row actions — all previously missing from the UI despite their backend endpoints already existing and going unused.
- **Claim workspace (`CRMClaimProfile.jsx`)** — converted from a single stacked-sections page into a real tabbed layout (Overview / Reports / Photos), with loading/error/retry states added throughout (previously just a bare `PageLoader` with no retry). The new **Photos tab** aggregates real photos across every report linked to the claim (via the existing `GET /reports/:id/photos` endpoint, the same one `ReportPhotoGallery` uses), with filters for Report, Category (dynamically populated from real `analysis.category` values), and Review Status — never fabricated data, and a report whose photos fail to load is skipped rather than failing the whole tab.
- **Real bug found and fixed:** duplicating a report set `editableContent` to the literal JavaScript `null` (the fresh draft's `content` field), which `SectionedReportEditor`'s `parseReportSections` (via `String(content)`) rendered as the literal text **"null"** in the editor — a real, visible data-integrity-looking bug, not a crash. Fixed by falling back to `''` at the one call site that didn't already guard against it (every other call site in `Dashboard.jsx` already did).

**Files touched:** `backend/routes/reports.js` (list-filter params, `/duplicate`, `/restore`, `createdByEmail`, `preArchiveStatus`), `backend/routes/crm.js` (claims list date/archived params, `archived` validator), `backend/services/crmService.js` (`getClaims` date-range + archived filtering), `frontend/src/services/api.js` (`reportsAPI.duplicate`/`.restore`), `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (filters panel, `RowActionsMenu`, Archive/Restore/Duplicate handlers, the null-content fix), `frontend/src/pages/CRM.jsx` (Claims search/date/archived UI, Edit/Archive/Delete actions, `NewClaimModal` edit mode), `frontend/src/pages/CRMClaimProfile.jsx` (tabs, Photos aggregation panel, retry states).

**Testing/verification — automated:** Backend 135/135 tests (unchanged — new endpoints are straightforward Firestore CRUD in the same style as existing, already-tested routes; live verification below was judged more valuable than mocking Firestore for these), 0 lint errors, 44 warnings (+1 over the 43 baseline — one new `catch(err)` following the codebase's existing accepted pattern). Frontend 15/15 tests, 0 lint errors, 15 warnings (unchanged baseline), build succeeds.

**Testing/verification — live** (real Anthropic API + real Firebase project, Playwright/Chromium, 2 throwaway Agency-tier accounts + one seeded CRM client/claim, all fully deleted afterward): **My Reports filters — 13/13** (Loss Type, Report Type, Claim #, Status, Created-date range, Organization, and Creator each independently isolated the correct seeded report(s); Clear Filters correctly restored the full list). **My Reports actions — 15/15** across a single clean run (Duplicate → correct " (Copy)" claim number in the list and a clean empty editor after the null-content fix; Archive → non-danger dialog, disappears from default view, reappears under the Archived filter with a Restore action; Restore → returns to the All view as a draft; permanent Delete → gone entirely; Download PDF → a real file downloads; Share → enabled and copies a real working link for a finalized report, correctly disabled with a tooltip for an unfinalized one). **Claims tab — 10/10** (search finds/excludes correctly, an empty-result state renders, date range matches by loss date, Edit persists a changed field, Archive/Restore/the "Show Archived" toggle all work). **Claim workspace — 7/7** (loads directly by URL, all three tabs present, Reports tab shows the linked report(s), Photos tab correctly aggregated 2 real, AI-analyzed, reviewer-pending photos from a real end-to-end generated report linked to the seeded claim — with working Report/Category/Review-status filters — and the workspace survives a direct page refresh). **Cross-user authorization — 5/5** (a second account gets a clean "Claim unavailable" on the first account's claim URL with zero data leaked, and never sees the first account's reports in its own My Reports list). **Mobile (390×844) — 5/5** (My Reports and the claim workspace both render with zero horizontal overflow, zero console/network errors). Several 429s from this session's own rapid repeated test iterations were encountered and resolved each time via a backend restart (clears the in-memory rate-limiter store) — the same well-documented, self-inflicted testing artifact from Phases 9-11, confirmed not a regression by clean re-runs; the one pre-existing, unrelated stuck `processing` report from 2026-07-29 was reconfirmed present and left untouched throughout.

**Not covered live:** a genuinely pre-Phase-6 legacy-shaped report (no `photos[]` array) was not specifically re-tested against the new filters/Duplicate/Archive actions this session — these all operate on fields (`lossType`, `reportType`, `status`, `claimNumber`) that exist identically on legacy reports, and Duplicate explicitly never touches `photos`/`imagePaths` either way, so no legacy-specific behavior was introduced, but this wasn't independently re-confirmed with a real synthesized legacy record. Pagination beyond a single page of results was not exercised (the seeded test data fit on one page).

**Status:** ✅ **Completed (2026-08-18)**
