## Phase 18 — Settings Completion

**Objective:** Add the missing Organization, Branding, and Data tabs, and expand Notifications to the full 6-toggle set.

**Reason this phase is required:** Item 37 (`PARTIALLY COMPLETE`) — only 5 of 7 spec'd tabs exist; no organization-level fields (industry/website/timezone) anywhere; Branding only exists gated inside Enterprise's White-Label view; Notifications has 3 of 6 toggles; no account data-export feature exists.

**Scope:** Frontend `Settings.jsx` + small backend additions (organization fields on user/org doc, data-export endpoint).

**Exact tasks:**
1. Add an Organization tab: Company Name, Website, Industry, Address, Timezone.
2. Add a Branding tab (or decide to keep branding Enterprise-only and document that as an intentional tier restriction — open question).
3. Expand Notifications to the full spec set: report completed, analysis completed, review requested, report approved, report shared, billing.
4. Add a Data tab: file-deletion controls, retention settings display, and an account-data export (currently entirely absent — confirm scope/format, e.g. a JSON export of the user's reports/claims metadata).

**Files/components likely affected:** `frontend/src/pages/Settings.jsx`, `backend/routes/users.js`.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** None blocking.

**Expected output:** Settings matches the spec's 7-tab structure with working fields.

**Acceptance criteria:** Organization fields persist and are usable elsewhere (e.g. report cover pages); all 6 notification toggles function; data export produces a real downloadable file.

**Testing/verification steps:** Fill in and save each new field; trigger each notification event and confirm the toggle actually gates it; run a data export and inspect its contents for accuracy.

**Risks/considerations:** Data export should be scoped carefully (what counts as "account data") — treat as a mini legal/compliance question, not just an engineering one, given Golden Rule #6 (security/compliance).

**Status:** Completed (2026-08-19). Both open questions below were resolved with the client before implementation, not guessed at: **#7 (Branding tier-gating)** — Branding is now a real tab visible to every tier; non-Enterprise accounts get Company Name (moved here from Profile) + a new Report Footer Text field, both wired into the actual PDF/DOCX/HTML export pipeline, plus an honest Enterprise upgrade prompt; Enterprise accounts get a summary card linking out to the existing `/white-label` portal rather than a second, duplicated branding surface (mirroring Phase 17's "summary tabs link out" precedent). Full logo/color/domain white-label stayed exactly as Enterprise-only as before — no entitlement was expanded. **#8 (Account-export scope)** — a single JSON export via `GET /api/users/export-data`: profile/settings, this account's own reports (metadata + full content + photo/document references, never re-embedding binary files), personal templates, owned CRM records, and safe API-key/webhook metadata (never raw keys/hashes/secrets); explicitly excludes `mfaSecret`/recovery-code hashes/`tokenVersion`/`stripeCustomerId`/API-key hashes/webhook signing secrets and any other account's data.

Also fixed, not just built new: the Phase-13-flagged defect where a non-enterprise user's own Company Name never actually appeared in exports (`hideFlacronBranding` only ever engaged for template branding, never a real personal/org company name) — now any resolved company name suppresses the generic "FlacronAI" branding. Export branding additionally now resolves from the ORGANIZATION (via `resolveOrganizationId`), not just the caller's own doc, so a team member's export correctly inherits the org's Settings rather than their own blank fields — a solo account is its own organization, so this is a no-op behavior change for every existing non-team account. Organization-field writes are gated server-side by the existing Phase 14 `canManageTeam` capability (Owner/Admin/Manager), with restricted roles getting a real read-only view, never a 403 page or a silently-broken form. The Data tab's "file-deletion controls" is a real, bounded action — `POST /api/reports/archived/delete-all` permanently deletes every already-archived report (Storage files included), deliberately scoped to reports the user already marked for removal via Archive, never touching drafts/finalized reports.

Verification: 207/207 backend unit tests pass (7 new for `notificationPrefs.js`), 0 lint errors either package, clean frontend production build. A 41/41-check isolated live-API test (an ephemeral Express app + real Firestore, emailService/aiService monkey-patched to no-op spies so no real email was ever sent and no real Anthropic call made) proved organization role-gating and cross-org isolation, the export-branding fix for both solo and team-member accounts, notification-preference sanitization, all 4 gated email hooks (`reportCompleted`/`analysisCompleted`/`reportApproved`/`reportShared`) genuinely toggling on and off — including inside the real (AI-mocked) generation pipeline — data-export scope/security, and the archived-reports bulk-delete. Real-browser (Playwright) verification covered every account type (solo Professional, solo Agency, Enterprise Owner/Admin/Manager, a restricted Adjuster, a suspended member, and a second cross-organization Enterprise Owner) with every check passing cleanly in at least one full run (most across several) — full tab walkthroughs, field persistence across reload, a real triggered file download, the delete-archived confirm/cancel flow, and mobile layout, all on the real running dev server. A pre-existing, already-flagged (Phase 16) sitewide UX defect — the cookie-consent banner blocking real clicks — had to be worked around in the test script itself (not fixed, out of this phase's scope). See `CONTEXT.md`'s "Current Active Phase" section for full detail, including the honest account of intermittent Playwright/dev-server timing flakiness encountered and how it was distinguished from a real defect.

## Phase 19 — Sharing Permissions, Expiry & Comments

**Objective:** Add permission levels and expiration to report sharing, and implement comment threads.

**Reason this phase is required:** Item 48 (`PARTIALLY COMPLETE`) — sharing is currently a permanent, binary public link with no View/Comment/Review distinction and no expiry; comments don't exist at all.

**Scope:** Backend share-token model extension + new comments feature (data model, endpoints, UI).

**Exact tasks:**
1. Extend the share-token model with an optional `expiresAt` (24h/7d/30d) and a `permission` level (View/Comment/Review).
2. Enforce expiry server-side on `GET /shared/:token`.
3. Design and implement a comments data model (anchored to a report section, per Phase 9's section model) with add/resolve actions.
4. Build the comment UI: highlight a section, add a comment, resolve a comment — visible to users with Comment/Review-level share access as well as the report owner/team.

**Files/components likely affected:** `backend/routes/reports.js` (share endpoints), new comments endpoints/collection, `frontend/src/pages/SharedReport.jsx`, `frontend/src/components/SectionedReportEditor.jsx` (comment anchoring).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Soft dependency on Phase 9 (comment anchoring is cleaner with a defined section model).

**Expected output:** Sharing becomes permission-aware and time-limited; reports support real collaborative review comments.

**Acceptance criteria:** An expired share link correctly denies access; a View-only share cannot add comments; a Comment/Review share can; resolved comments are visually distinguished from open ones.

**Testing/verification steps:** Create shares at each permission level and expiry, verify enforcement of both; add and resolve comments as different permission levels.

**Risks/considerations:** Comment notifications would naturally pair with Phase 20 (notifications) — sequence these two phases close together if both are prioritized.

**Amendment (2026-08-16 full-spec audit) — 2 additional tasks:**
5. **"Invite User" direct sharing, distinct from the anonymous permission-level link above.** The spec (§28) explicitly separates "Invite User" (a named account gets access, presumably notified) from "Generate Secure Link" (the anonymous, permission-leveled link this phase's original tasks 1-2 already cover) — these are two different mechanisms, not one feature with two names. Add a share-by-email flow that looks up or invites the recipient (can reuse Phase 14's team-invite email pattern) and grants them report-level access distinct from team membership.
6. **Supervisor review-request workflow** (spec §30, Agency Workflow: "Adjuster generates report → Reviewer receives review request → Reviewer comments/edits → Report approved → Export"). This is more than a Comment-level share — it implies an explicit "Request Review" action that notifies a specific reviewer (ties directly into Phase 20's notification center) and likely a distinct report state (e.g. `NEEDS_REVIEW` from Phase 7's status vocabulary, assigned to a specific reviewer) rather than just an open share link. Coordinate closely with Phase 14 (roles — who counts as a "reviewer") and Phase 20 (the notification itself).

**Status:** ✅ Completed (2026-08-19, session 24), per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 19 only"). Full detail in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 19 entry and `CONTEXT.md`'s "Current Active Phase" section; summary below.

**Implementation summary:**
- **Sharing:** the legacy single-token, view-only, non-expiring link (`shareToken`/`sharedAt` on the report doc, `POST/DELETE /:id/share`, `GET /shared/:token`) is untouched for backward compatibility. A new top-level `reportShares` collection (chosen over a subcollection so a public token lookup stays a single-field equality query, requiring no new Firestore index) backs `POST/GET /:id/shares` + `DELETE /:id/shares/:shareId`: View/Comment/Review permission levels, optional 24h/7d/30d server-computed expiry, revocation, all enforced live on every `GET /shared/:token` call (never cached). View links stay finalized-only (Golden Rule #3); Comment/Review links may target a draft, with the shared page always showing an explicit "DRAFT — pending review" banner (client-confirmed design decision).
- **Direct Invite User sharing:** `POST/DELETE /:id/share/invite[/:uid]` grants a named, *existing* FlacronAI account (looked up by email) per-report View/Comment/Review access via a new `assignedUsers`/`assignedUserUids` pair on the report doc — independent of team membership, so it also works cross-organization. Scoped to existing accounts only this phase (client-confirmed) — inviting a stranger by email with no account yet, mirroring `teams.js`'s pending-invite flow, is a documented follow-up.
- **Supervisor review requests:** `POST /:id/request-review` (owner assigns an in-organization reviewer — cross-org rejected with `REVIEWER_NOT_IN_ORG`), `POST /:id/review-response` (assigned reviewer declines or asks for changes; approval itself reuses the existing `POST /:id/approve`, now reachable by a `review`-tier grantee), and `GET /reports/assigned-to-me` (array-contains query strictly on the caller's own uid — never the organization's report pool). The reviewer role's capability flags already existed from Phase 14; the missing piece was purely the per-report access grant.
- **Access model:** a new `backend/utils/reportAccess.js` (`getReportAccess`/`hasReportAccess`) is the single source of truth for what a non-owner may do on a specific report — deliberately independent of the grantee's own account role/tier (every solo account resolves to "owner" of itself in `orgRoles.js`, which must never leak onto someone else's report). Ownership checks were surgically widened (not blanket-loosened) on exactly `GET /:id`, `PUT /:id`, and `POST /:id/approve`; `requireCanEditReports`/`requireCanApprove` middleware was removed from those two routes and replaced with an inline check that preserves the exact prior owner-path behavior. Export/download, delete, duplicate, restore, and the AI-assist routes stay strictly owner-only — a deliberate scope trim, not an oversight.
- **Comments:** a new `reports/{id}/comments` subcollection (add/view/reply/resolve/reopen), anchored to a section by a content-based title slug (`slugifySectionTitle`, duplicated identically in `frontend/src/utils/reportSections.js` for client-side grouping) rather than position, so a comment survives reordering — only a literal rename/removal breaks the anchor, falling back to a "General" bucket with the original title kept for context. An anonymous Comment/Review-permission link can add comments (capturing a guest display name, never an authUid); only Review-permission (anonymous or authenticated) or the owner can resolve/reopen — an anonymous link never gets approve/reject (Golden Rule #3 reserves that attestation for an identified reviewer).
- **Notifications:** `reviewRequested` (defined but never wired since Phase 18) is now genuinely triggered on `request-review`, gated by the reviewer's own preference; a new `sendReviewResponseEmail` notifies the owner on decline/changes-requested (reusing the `reviewRequested` toggle for both directions of the review lifecycle — a deliberate, documented scope choice); a new `sendReportAccessGrantedEmail` notifies a direct-invite recipient, gated by their own `reportShared` preference.
- **Security:** no raw share token is ever written to an audit-log `meta` (live-verified); comment bodies are control-character-stripped and length-capped (`sanitizeCommentBody`) and rendered only as plain React text (never `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`); tokens are random UUIDs (no enumeration surface); `assigned-to-me` and every widened route return `404 NOT_FOUND` (never a data-revealing 403) to a party with no grant at all.
- **Frontend:** `ShareReportModal.jsx` (link creation with permission/expiry, list/revoke, Invite User) and `CommentsPanel.jsx` (shared between `ReportPreviewPage.jsx`'s authenticated view and `SharedReport.jsx`'s anonymous view, with the same stale-response-guard pattern Phase 17's `AuditLogViewer.jsx` established) are new, reusable components. `ReportPreviewPage.jsx` gained Share/Request-Review buttons, a review-pending banner, and Approve/Return-for-Changes/Decline actions for an assigned reviewer, all gated by the server-returned `myAccess` field (never assumed client-side). `SharedReport.jsx` gained expired/revoked states and the draft banner. `Dashboard.jsx`'s home view gained a small "Shared With You" widget (only rendered when non-empty).

**Verification:** 217/217 backend unit tests pass (10 new, `report-access.test.js`), 0 backend lint errors (59 pre-existing warnings, unchanged baseline). 17/17 frontend unit tests pass (2 new, section-anchor slug stability), 0 frontend lint errors (14 pre-existing warnings, unchanged baseline), a clean production build. **No browser automation tool was available this session** (same documented gap as Phases 7/13/14/15) — frontend UI was verified by code review + the clean build only. A 51/51-check isolated live-API test (an ephemeral Express app on its own port + the real Firestore project, `emailService.js`'s send functions monkey-patched to no-op spies before `reports.js` was first required, so no real SES email was ever sent) covered every permission level and expiry option, direct invites and anonymous links (including guest comments), expired/revoked access, add/resolve/reopen comments, the review-request/response/approve lifecycle, cross-organization rejection, a suspended member blocked entirely at the auth layer, server-side denial via direct API calls (never just a hidden button) for view-only/comment-only/no-grant callers, and confirmed no raw token ever appears in an audit-log `meta`. One test (cross-org reviewer rejection) initially failed because an earlier assertion in the same run had already left a pending review request on the same report, so the correct `REVIEW_ALREADY_PENDING` guard fired before the org check could be exercised — a test-ordering bug, not a product bug; re-run in isolation on a fresh report, it passed. All seeded data (9 users, 3 reports, their `reportShares`/`comments`/`auditLogs`) was deleted afterward with a verified zero-residue re-check.

## Phase 20 — Notifications Center & Global Search

**Objective:** Build an in-app notification center and a global CMD/CTRL+K search.

**Reason this phase is required:** Item 44 (`NOT IMPLEMENTED`) — no notification bell/feed/collection exists, only ephemeral toasts; Item 45 (`NOT STARTED`) — no global search exists anywhere.

**Scope:** New backend `notifications` collection + write-triggers on key events; new frontend notification bell/feed component; new global search component with a lightweight cross-entity backend search endpoint.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Add a `notifications` Firestore collection and write entries on: analysis completed/failed, report generated, report ready for review, review requested, report approved, export completed, team invitation, subscription issue.
2. Build a notification bell + dropdown/feed UI in the top bar, with read/unread state.
3. Build a global search component (CMD/CTRL+K trigger) querying across claim number, insured, report, address, photo, team member.
4. Add a lightweight backend search endpoint (simple prefix/contains queries against Firestore, or a light client-side aggregation of already-loaded lists if full-text search infra isn't justified yet — flag as a scope decision).

**Files/components likely affected:** New backend notification-writing hooks across `reports.js`/`teams.js`/`payment.js`, new `frontend/src/components/NotificationCenter.jsx`, new `frontend/src/components/GlobalSearch.jsx`, `frontend/src/components/Navbar.jsx` / `Dashboard.jsx` top bar.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Best done after Phases 12/14/17 exist (claims, team, org data to search/notify about are more complete by then).

**Expected output:** Users get persistent, actionable notifications and can find things quickly across the app.

**Acceptance criteria:** Notifications appear for every listed event and persist across sessions until read; global search returns correct cross-entity results within a reasonable latency; CMD/CTRL+K opens the search from anywhere in the authenticated app.

**Testing/verification steps:** Trigger each notification-generating event and confirm it appears; search for a known claim number, report, and team member name and confirm correct results.

**Risks/considerations:** Full-text search at scale may eventually need a dedicated search service (e.g. Algolia/Typesense) if Firestore's query limitations become a problem — start simple, flag if scale becomes an issue.

**Status:** ✅ Completed (2026-08-19, session 25), per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 20 only"). Full detail in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 20 entry and `CONTEXT.md`'s "Current Active Phase" section; summary below.

**Implementation summary:**
- **Notifications data model:** a new top-level `notifications` collection (`backend/utils/notificationService.js`) with `{uid, type, title, body, link, meta, read, createdAt, readAt}`. Unread state is tracked via a plain counter (`users/{uid}.unreadNotificationCount`, `FieldValue.increment`) rather than a live `.where('read','==',false)` count query — O(1) reads for the bell badge instead of an ever-growing scan. The bounded-window/no-composite-index constraint from Phase 19 (no ability to deploy new indexes this session) is handled the same way as `teams.js`'s `RECENT_ACTIVITY_WINDOW`/Phase 19's `assigned-to-me`: a single-field `where('uid','==',uid).limit(200)` read, sorted and paginated in JS, with a `windowCapped` flag if the true count exceeds that window.
- **The 8 spec'd events, each wired at its real trigger site, reusing Phase 18's preference gate wherever a matching key exists:** analysis completed/failed and report generated (both fire from the real background pipeline, `photoJobService.js` — "report generated" and "ready for review" are the same instant in this codebase's state machine, since a report becomes `draft` exactly when generation completes; there is no separate later moment to notify a second time for the same transition), review requested + review declined/changes-requested (`reports.js`, reusing the `reviewRequested` toggle both directions per Phase 19's own precedent), report approved (both the approving actor and, when a Phase 19 grantee completes someone else's review request, the owner), report access granted via direct invite (bonus — reuses `reportShared`, since it's the same conceptual event Phase 19 already gates), export completed (`POST /:id/export` — deliberately **ungated**, since no Phase 18 toggle covers it and it's a direct result of the caller's own click, closer to a receipt than an alert) and team invitation (`teams.js` — also ungated, matching the pre-existing email's own ungated posture; only fires when the invited email already belongs to an existing FlacronAI account, since a brand-new invitee has no uid yet to notify), and subscription issue (`payment.js`'s `invoice.payment_failed` webhook handler, reusing `billing`, with a `dedupeKey` on the Stripe invoice id as defense-in-depth against a redelivered webhook — Stripe's own `processedWebhooks` idempotency check already covers the common case). `analysis_failed` has no dedicated Phase 18 toggle either — deliberately reuses `analysisCompleted` rather than fabricating a 7th key, the same reuse-not-fabricate posture Phase 18/19 already established.
- **Deduplication:** a `dedupeKey` option on `notifyUser()`, when given, writes the notification via `.doc(dedupeKey).create()` (fails on a genuine duplicate, silently absorbed) instead of `.add()`. Used for the two sites with a real redelivery/double-submit risk (the Stripe webhook, a double-clicked team invite); every other event site already has its own natural one-shot guard upstream (Phase 19's `REVIEW_ALREADY_PENDING` check, one approve/export call, one pipeline run per generation) — documented at each call site rather than re-derived generically.
- **Bell/feed API:** `GET /api/notifications` (bounded+paginated, `unreadCount`, `hasMore`), `POST /api/notifications/:id/read` (transactional, ownership-checked — someone else's notification id is a flat 404, never a data-revealing 403; clamps the counter at 0), `POST /api/notifications/mark-all-read` (one bounded Firestore batch, capped at 500 writes — an account with genuinely more than 500 unread needs a second click rather than an uncapped write loop). All three are session-only (`authenticateToken`, no API-key scope), matching `users.js`'s own precedent for UI-only features.
- **Global search:** `GET /api/search?q=` (`backend/routes/search.js`), scoped EXACTLY to Phase 19's own access model — the caller's own reports (`userId==uid`) plus reports specifically assigned to them (`assignedUserUids array-contains uid`) — never any wider organization pool, exactly matching this phase's own "never expose the entire organization's report pool" requirement. No Firestore full-text search exists, so this is a bounded, permission-scoped read (capped at 300 docs per source) followed by an in-memory case-insensitive substring match — explicitly NOT the same thing as "client-side filtering" (which would mean shipping everything to the browser and filtering there): the scoping and matching both happen server-side, and only matches are ever returned. Searches claim number/insured/address/loss type and, within each already-authorized report, photo filename/AI category/location — all effectively the same underlying `reports` collection, since photos are a sub-array of the report doc, not a separate collection (that's Phase 22's job). Team-member results are additionally gated by `canManageTeam`/`canViewAllProfiles` (a restricted role gets an empty `team` group, not the roster) and matched on email/role only — enterpriseTeams docs don't carry a display name, the same limitation the existing `GET /teams/members` roster already lives with, so this doesn't regress anything. A query under 2 characters returns empty groups rather than scanning.
- **Frontend:** `NotificationBell.jsx` (poll every 60s + refetch on open, mark-one/mark-all, "Load more" pagination, a monotonic-requestId stale-response guard matching Phase 17/19's `AuditLogViewer.jsx`/`CommentsPanel.jsx` precedent) and `GlobalSearch.jsx` (CMD/CTRL+K global listener + a custom `flacron:open-search` DOM event so a visible button can open it without lifting state through unrelated layouts; 300ms debounce + a genuine `AbortController` cancel — not just a requestId guard — so a fast typist's stale request stops costing backend work too; grouped, keyboard-navigable results with plain-text `<mark>` highlighting, never `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`; up to 5 recent searches in `localStorage`). Both are mounted in `Navbar.jsx` (desktop + a mobile-reachable row next to the hamburger button) — which every authenticated page already renders (`Dashboard.jsx`, `ReportPreviewPage.jsx`, `Settings.jsx`, etc.) — and `GlobalSearch` is additionally mounted once at the `App.jsx` root (gated on `isAuthenticated`) so CMD/CTRL+K works from literally any authenticated route, including `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s separate sidebar layout, which also got its own visible bell + search button wired into its existing top bar.
- **Security/isolation:** every new route requires a valid session token (`GET /notifications`/`/search` with no token: 401, verified live); a notification belonging to a different uid 404s rather than leaking existence; search never returns another organization's or another user's reports, photos, or team roster (verified live in both directions — org A's owner gets zero results for org B's claim number, and a stranger with zero grant gets zero results for a report only assigned to org A's reviewer); notification `meta` is a minimal `{reportId, claimNumber}` projection, never a raw token/secret.

**Verification:** 223/223 backend unit tests pass (6 new, `notification-service.test.js`, covering `buildNotification`'s pure copy-generation logic for all 10 types — the actual Firestore read/write/counter behavior is covered by the live-API pass below, the same test-responsibility split Phase 19 established for `reportAccess.js`), 0 backend lint errors (59 pre-existing warnings, unchanged baseline). 17/17 frontend unit tests pass (unchanged — no new pure-logic frontend module this phase, matching Phase 19's own precedent of not unit-testing UI components), 0 frontend lint errors (14 pre-existing warnings, unchanged baseline), a clean production build. **No browser automation tool was available this session** (same documented gap as Phases 7/13/14/15/19) — the frontend UI layer was verified by code review + the clean build only, not an actual rendered page. A 35/35-check isolated live-API test (an ephemeral Express app on its own port + the real Firestore project, every `emailService.js` send function and `aiService.js`'s vision/generation calls monkey-patched to safe mocks before the consuming routers were first required, so no real SES email and no real Anthropic call was ever made) exercised: the real background pipeline genuinely firing `analysis_completed`/`report_completed` on success and `analysis_failed` on a real generation failure (not a simulated write); paginated/bounded notification listing with an accurate `unreadCount`; mark-one-read (idempotent on a second call, 404 for another user's notification) and mark-all-read (counter reaches exactly 0); a true dedupe-key collision producing exactly one document; team-invitation notifications firing only for an already-existing account and never erroring on an email with no account; report-approval and export-completion notifications firing from the real routes; and the full search matrix (claim number, partial insured name, partial address, photo filename/category, team member by email, a restricted role's empty team group, a reviewer's assigned-not-owned report, and double-checked zero-result cross-organization/stranger isolation) plus both new routes' 401 on no token. Two checks initially failed and were root-caused as test-timing artifacts, not product bugs: (1) a malformed seed photo missing `severity`/`observation` fields tripped a real Firestore constraint (`.update()` rejects `undefined`) — confirmed by reading `aiService.js`'s own per-photo normalizer (`severity: VALID_SEVERITIES.has(p?.severity) ? p.severity : 'Unknown'`) that production analysis output can never actually produce this shape, so the seed was fixed, not the product; (2) two fire-and-forget notification writes (team-invite, report-approve) involve 2-3 sequential Firestore round-trips before resolving, and the test's fixed check-delay initially ran ahead of them — confirmed via temporary debug instrumentation that the writes succeeded correctly, just later than assumed, and the delay was lengthened rather than the response ever being made to block on them (that would contradict this codebase's own established fire-and-forget philosophy for exactly this class of side-effect). All seeded data (6 users, 3 reports, 2 team-invite docs, and every notification they generated) was deleted afterward via the script's own cleanup step, independently re-verified with a zero-residue Firestore query pass; `git status` shows only the expected new/modified files. **Client has instructed (standing, from prior sessions): do not commit, push, or deploy anything. Awaiting explicit instruction before starting Phase 21 or any further phase.**

## Phase 21 — Onboarding Flow

**Objective:** Build the post-signup onboarding flow for regular (non-enterprise-subdomain) signups.

**Reason this phase is required:** Item 50 confirms no generic onboarding exists — only `EnterpriseOnboarding.jsx` for enterprise-subdomain signups; regular signups go straight to `/dashboard`.

**Scope:** New frontend onboarding flow, minor backend field additions (user-type, expected volume) on the user doc.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Build a welcome screen shown once after first signup.
2. Add a "what best describes you?" step (Independent Adjuster/Adjusting Company/Insurance Company/Inspector/Contractor/Other) — persist to the user doc (useful later for Phase 15 analytics segmentation and Phase 23 marketing personas).
3. Add a "how many reports per month?" step (1–10/11–50/51–200/200+).
4. Add an optional invite-team step (reuses Phase 14's invite flow).
5. End with a "Generate Your First Report" CTA leading directly into the wizard.

**Files/components likely affected:** New `frontend/src/pages/Onboarding.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/Auth.jsx` (redirect target after signup), `backend/routes/users.js` (persist new profile fields).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Phase 3 (auth flow) and Phase 14 (team invite, for the optional step).

**Expected output:** New users get a guided first-run experience instead of landing cold in the wizard.

**Acceptance criteria:** Every new signup passes through onboarding exactly once (not shown again on subsequent logins); skipping is possible where marked optional; ends correctly at the report wizard.

**Testing/verification steps:** Sign up as a new user, complete/skip each step, verify persisted profile fields, confirm onboarding doesn't reappear on next login.

**Risks/considerations:** Keep this light — the spec explicitly scopes it to 5 short screens; avoid scope creep into a heavier "setup wizard."

**Status:** ✅ Completed (2026-08-19, session 26), per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 21 only"). Full detail in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 21 entry and `CONTEXT.md`'s "Current Active Phase" section; summary below.

**Implementation summary:**
- **Persistence model:** 4 new fields live directly on the existing `users/{uid}` doc (no new collection) — `onboardingCompleted`, `onboardingStep` (0-4), `onboardingUserType`, `onboardingMonthlyVolume`, plus `onboardingCompletedAt`/`onboardingSkipReason` once finished. They're set to `false`/`0`/`null`/`null` ONLY at the exact moment `GET /api/users/profile`'s existing auto-create transaction (Phase 3) first creates a brand-new doc — an **existing** account's doc already exists by the time this phase shipped, so it never passes through that branch and simply has no `onboardingCompleted` field at all. Every gate below treats that absence exactly like `true` (already done) — only a literal `false` means "genuinely needs it" — which is what makes "existing users must not be incorrectly forced through onboarding" hold with zero backfill/migration risk.
- **Backend (`backend/utils/onboarding.js` + 2 new routes in `backend/routes/users.js`):** `POST /api/users/onboarding/step` validates the 6-value user-type enum, the 4-value volume enum, and clamps `step` to 0-4 — critically, it also enforces prerequisites SERVER-SIDE (step ≥2 requires a user-type already on file, step ≥3 requires volume already on file), so a direct API call can't jump ahead of what a disabled Next button already prevents in the UI. `POST /api/users/onboarding/complete` requires both mandatory answers already on file (400 `ONBOARDING_INCOMPLETE` otherwise) unless called with `{skipped:true}` — the one deliberate bypass, used only by the enterprise-subdomain flow (see below). Both routes operate exclusively on `req.user.uid` with no id/target parameter anywhere in either body, so cross-account modification is structurally impossible, not just permission-checked. Both are also idempotent once already completed (`{alreadyCompleted:true}`, never an error or a silent state regression) — the multi-tab case this phase calls out explicitly: a stale tab's Next/Skip click after onboarding finished elsewhere no-ops cleanly instead of resurrecting or corrupting completed state.
- **Frontend gate (`ProtectedRoute.jsx`):** a single new check — `userProfile.onboardingCompleted === false` (and not already on `/onboarding`) → redirect to `/onboarding` — added to the SAME central gate every authenticated route already passes through (Dashboard, Settings, report preview, CRM, etc.), so direct URL access to any protected page while incomplete redirects back to onboarding, not just `/dashboard`. `Onboarding.jsx` itself handles the inverse case (already done → bounce to `/dashboard`) so `/onboarding` never needs a special-cased prop beyond `skipOnboardingGate` (to avoid redirecting to itself).
- **The 5-step wizard (`frontend/src/pages/Onboarding.jsx`):** Welcome → "what best describes you" (6 icon cards) → monthly volume (4 options) → optional Invite Team (only rendered when `tier === 'enterprise'` — reuses Phase 14's exact `teamsAPI.invite`/`teamsAPI.getRoles` calls, server-side-enforced independently since `GET /teams/roles`/`POST /teams/invite` already 403 a non-enterprise caller regardless of what the UI renders) → "Generate Your First Report" CTA. Every Next/Back/Skip transition is persisted to the server (not just local state) before advancing, and `refreshProfile()` is called after each one so a refresh, another tab, or `ProtectedRoute`'s own gate all see the exact same server truth this page just wrote. A `ready` gate blocks any render until the server's step/answers have been synced into local state once, specifically to prevent a flash of step 0 for an account resuming mid-flow at a later step. Step numbering (0-4) is fixed regardless of team-invite eligibility — the step is omitted from the visible sequence for a non-enterprise account rather than renumbered, so `onboardingStep`'s meaning never depends on tier.
- **The CTA → wizard handoff:** the final button calls `POST .../complete` then navigates to `/dashboard?startWizard=1` — `Dashboard.jsx` reads that query param the same way it already reads the pre-existing `?openReport=` deep-link (clear the URL, then `setActiveView('generate')`), landing directly on the report wizard's first step with zero new routing.
- **Enterprise-subdomain exclusion:** Phase 21's own Scope line explicitly limits this to "regular (non-enterprise-subdomain) signups" — `EnterpriseOnboarding.jsx` (which has its own portal-branded first-run experience) now calls `getProfile()` then `completeOnboarding({skipped:true, reason:'enterprise_subdomain'})` immediately after a successful register, before ever navigating to `/dashboard` — the `getProfile()`-first ordering matters here for the same reason `Auth.jsx`'s existing `persistSignupProfileDetails` already does it that way: racing ahead of the auto-create transaction would leave the doc missing its other defaults.
- **Google vs. email/password consistency:** deliberately no signup-provider-specific logic anywhere in this phase — the gate lives entirely in `ProtectedRoute`/`AuthContext`, which reads the same `userProfile.onboardingCompleted` field regardless of how the account was created, and `GET /profile`'s auto-create (the only place the field is ever initialized) runs identically for both.

**Verification:** 228/228 backend unit tests pass (5 new, `onboarding.test.js`, covering the enum/step-bounds pure validators — the actual Firestore read/write/prerequisite-enforcement behavior is covered by the live-API pass below, the same test-responsibility split established since Phase 19), 0 backend lint errors (59 pre-existing warnings, unchanged baseline). 17/17 frontend unit tests pass (unchanged — no new pure-logic frontend module this phase), 0 frontend lint errors (14 pre-existing warnings, unchanged baseline), a clean production build. A 30/30-check isolated live-API test (ephemeral Express app + the real Firestore project, email sends mocked) covered every enum/step/prerequisite validation path, the idempotent-after-completion behavior, the enterprise-subdomain skip path, and confirmed `GET /teams/roles` genuinely 403s a non-enterprise caller (proving the Team step's eligibility gate isn't just a frontend choice not to render it). **Real-browser verification was available this session** (Playwright's Chromium was already installed, unlike the documented gap in Phases 7/13/14/15/19/20) — a 16/16-check Playwright pass against the actual dev servers (a genuinely fresh Vite instance this session started; the already-running backend on port 3000, left untouched per the established "don't manipulate a shared dev server" precedent, was confirmed to already be serving this phase's exact new routes) drove real Firebase-Admin-created, pre-verified throwaway accounts through: a brand-new signup landing on `/onboarding`, every step forward/back with selections retained, the team step correctly never appearing for a starter-tier account, a mid-flow refresh resuming at the exact same step with no flash, direct URL access to `/settings` while incomplete redirecting back, completion handing off cleanly into the real report wizard, `/onboarding` never showing again after completion, an existing (pre-Phase-21, no onboarding fields) account landing straight on `/dashboard`, and a mobile (390×844) viewport pass — zero console errors and zero failed network requests across every scenario. Google-provider signup itself could not be safely automated (a real interactive OAuth consent flow isn't something to script with a throwaway account) — verified instead by code review confirming the gate is provider-agnostic by construction, per the "Google vs. email/password consistency" point above. Two real issues were found and fixed during this pass: (1) `persistStep`/`handleFinish` didn't check the `alreadyCompleted` flag the backend can return, so a stale tab after another tab finished onboarding would have kept advancing a step that no longer meant anything instead of redirecting away — fixed to redirect on that flag; (2) the disabled Next/Back buttons had no visual disabled state at all (`btn-primary`/`btn-secondary` don't bundle one), which a screenshot caught immediately but no automated check would have — fixed with explicit `disabled:opacity-50 disabled:cursor-not-allowed` classes. A third apparent issue (several screenshots looking washed-out/low-contrast) was investigated and disproven as a real bug: a follow-up check with a 1-second settle delay before screenshotting rendered crisply — the framer-motion step-transition fade-in (180ms) simply hadn't finished at the exact instant those screenshots were taken, a test-script timing artifact, not a rendering defect. All seeded data (Firestore docs + real Firebase Auth accounts) was deleted afterward via the script's own cleanup step, independently re-verified with a zero-residue query against both Firestore and Firebase Auth; the frontend dev server instance this session started was stopped afterward, the backend server (pre-existing, not started by this session) was left running untouched; `git status` shows only the expected new/modified files. **Client has instructed (standing, from prior sessions): do not commit, push, or deploy anything. Awaiting explicit instruction before starting Phase 22 or any further phase.**

## Phase 22 — Photo Analysis Library (/photos)

**Objective:** Build a standalone cross-account photo library/search page.

**Reason this phase is required:** Item 31 (`NOT IMPLEMENTED`) — no such page exists; photos are only browsable within a single report's upload step.

**Scope:** New frontend page + backend query endpoint across a user/org's photos.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Add a backend endpoint to query photos across all of a user's/org's reports with filters (claim, date, location/category from Phase 8's taxonomy, status).
2. Build a `/photos` page with grid view and filters.
3. Click-through to expand a photo's full analysis (reusing Phase 8's per-photo review UI).

**Files/components likely affected:** New `frontend/src/pages/PhotoLibrary.jsx`, new backend query endpoint (likely in `reports.js` or a new `photos.js`).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Phase 7 and 8 (per-photo records with structured analysis must exist to search/filter/display meaningfully).

**Expected output:** A real cross-account photo search experience.

**Acceptance criteria:** Filters return correct results; clicking a photo opens its full analysis consistent with the per-report review UI.

**Testing/verification steps:** Search/filter across a test account with photos spanning multiple claims/categories/statuses; verify result accuracy.

**Risks/considerations:** Pagination/virtualization needed for accounts with large photo volumes — do not load all results unpaginated.

**Status:** ✅ Completed (2026-08-19, session 27), per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 22 only"). Full detail in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 22 entry; summary below.

**Implementation summary:**
- **Data model constraint:** no dedicated `photos` Firestore collection exists — every photo lives inside its owning report's `photos[]` array (or a legacy flat `imagePaths[]`). `GET /api/photos` (`backend/routes/photos.js`) does a bounded, permission-scoped scan of the caller's own + Phase-19-assigned reports (mirroring Phase 20's `search.js` access model exactly — never a wider organization pool), flattens/redacts each into a library entry via a new pure module (`backend/utils/photoLibrary.js`), then filters/searches/sorts/paginates that already-authorized in-memory window. Pagination uses an opaque offset-encoded cursor (bounded, `truncated`-flagged if a real account ever exceeds the scan cap) rather than a true incremental Firestore cursor — a deliberate, documented tradeoff given the data model, consistent with this codebase's own accepted fragility notes (Phase 27).
- **Server-side permission enforcement (`canReview`):** computed per-entry, never trusted from the client — owner path additionally requires `hasCapability(req.user, 'canEditReports')` (so a restricted team role stays read-only even on a report they Firestore-level "own"), assignee path requires exactly the Phase 19 'review' tier.
- **Three pre-existing endpoints fixed as a direct Phase 22 dependency:** `GET /:id/photos` and `GET /:id/photos/:photoId/image` previously 404'd for any non-owner (even a legitimate Phase 19 assignee) — tightened to `getReportAccess`, matching `GET /:id`'s existing model. `PUT /:id/photos/:photoId/review` had owner-only middleware stacked on a hardcoded owner-only service check — rewritten to the same dual-path pattern (owner+`canEditReports`, OR assignee with 'review' tier) `PUT /:id` already uses. A raw-Storage-path leak in `GET /:id` and the review PUT's response (`objectPath`/`thumbnailPath`/`originalPath`/`imagePaths` returned verbatim) was also closed — the frontend never read these fields.
- **Frontend (`frontend/src/pages/PhotoLibrary.jsx`, route `/photos`):** filters (claim/location/category/analysis-status/included-excluded/date range), debounced search, sort, live result count, Clear Filters; a responsive grid with cursor-based "Load More" and lazy thumbnail fetching bounded to loaded pages (no `react-window` added — judged unnecessary per Phase 27's own "confirm lazy/paginated is sufficient" guidance). The expanded photo view reuses **Phase 8's exact review UI**, extracted verbatim from Dashboard.jsx's `ReportPhotoGallery` into a new shared `frontend/src/components/PhotoReview.jsx` (zero behavior change to the existing report editor — it now imports the same component), generalized with a `canReview` prop for the read-only case. Legacy/missing-thumbnail photos show an honest "unavailable" placeholder. Nav entries added to `Navbar.jsx` and `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s sidebar.

**Verification:** 243/243 backend unit tests pass (15 new, `photo-library.test.js`), 0 backend lint errors (59 pre-existing warnings, unchanged). 17/17 frontend unit tests pass (unchanged), 0 frontend lint errors (14 pre-existing warnings, unchanged), a clean production build. A 50/50-check real-browser Playwright pass against a full seeded account matrix (owner with mixed categories/statuses/an excluded photo/a needs-attention photo/a failed upload/a legacy report/a 35-photo pagination-volume report; a view-tier assignee confirmed read-only both in UI and server-side; a review-tier assignee confirmed their edit genuinely persists; a restricted-role owner confirmed read-only despite Firestore-level ownership; an empty account; a cross-organization stranger confirmed fully isolated via direct API IDOR checks; a suspended team member blocked at the auth layer; an unauthenticated direct-URL redirect) covered every filter/search/sort/pagination/keyboard-accessibility/mobile-viewport path with zero console errors and zero failed requests. Four real product bugs (the two owner-only 404s, the review dual-path gap, the raw-path leak, and an initial capability-blind `canReview` derivation) were found and fixed before this pass completed clean; several apparent failures were investigated and confirmed to be test-script-only artifacts (fake seed Storage paths, a Playwright selector collision with Navbar's mobile toggle, a `waitForResponse` predicate not accounting for the versioned `/api/v1` axios base, and React 18 StrictMode's dev-only double-invoked mount effect — an already-documented, production-safe pattern in this codebase). All seeded data (7 Firebase Auth accounts, 6 Firestore reports, 18 Storage objects) was deleted and independently re-verified as zero-residue; temporary verification scripts deleted from `backend/`; `git status` shows only the expected new/modified files. **Client has instructed (standing, from prior sessions): do not commit, push, or deploy anything. Awaiting explicit instruction before starting Phase 23 or any further phase.**

## Phase 23 — Public Marketing Pages & Copy Consistency

**Objective:** Build the missing Features/Photo Analysis/Solutions public pages, complete Home page gaps, and fix copy-accuracy issues site-wide.

**Reason this phase is required:** Items 10, 11, 12 (`NOT IMPLEMENTED`), Item 9 (`PARTIALLY COMPLETE` — missing Photo Analysis section, only 6 of 9 feature cards, only 3 of 6 How It Works steps, wrong CTA text in one section), Item 13 (`NEEDS FIX` — Enterprise CTA wording, tier-photo-limit copy inaccuracy), Item 1/50 (inconsistent "FLACRON ENGINE" branding — three different name variants in use), Item 37 (Security.jsx's "Login History" overclaim, addressed in Phase 17 but confirm here too if not yet done).

**Scope (corrected 2026-08-16 — see amendment):** Primarily frontend marketing pages, **plus backend-generated document/prompt text** where engine-naming or banned terminology appears there too (task 5 already implied this — "generated documents" — but the Scope line above originally said "no backend change expected," which was inconsistent with its own task list; fixed here).

**Exact tasks:**
1. Build `/features` with the 13 spec'd sections, each with a real product screenshot.
2. Build `/photo-analysis` with the spec'd feature list and CTA.
3. Build `/solutions` with cards linking to per-vertical pages (Independent Adjusters, Adjusting Firms, Insurance Carriers, TPAs, Inspection Companies, Restoration Companies, Contractors) — these per-vertical pages can start as lightweight, mostly-shared-template pages differentiated by persona-specific copy.
4. Complete Home.jsx: add the missing Photo Analysis section (image grid/counter/progress bar), expand feature cards to 9, expand How It Works to 6 steps, add the "Analyze the Entire Inspection" section below the product screenshot, fix the "Actual Product" section's CTA text to "Try It Free".
5. Standardize on one consistent name for the engine across the entire site **and generated documents** (resolve the "Flacron Engine" vs. "FlacronAI Engine" vs. "FLACRON ENGINE" inconsistency — pick one casing/form and apply everywhere, **including `properPdfGenerator.js`/`documentGenerator.js`/`aiService.js`'s prompt and template text, not just frontend copy** — a live QA screenshot from Phase 6 this session showed "Prepared With the Flacron Engine" baked into the PDF cover page, confirming this isn't purely a frontend concern).
6. Fix Pricing.jsx: Enterprise CTA to "Talk to Sales", align tier feature copy with actual entitlements (do not claim tier-specific photo-processing limits that aren't server-enforced, unless Phase 1's changes make this differentiation real). See the new Enterprise-pricing-model Open Question below — resolve that first, since it affects whether this task is "fix the CTA wording" or "add back a public price."
7. Verify/update Security.jsx's claims are accurate after Phase 17 (audit-log viewer) ships, or soften the copy until then.
8. **(Added 2026-08-16)** Sitewide banned-terminology sweep — grep and manually review every public and authenticated page (not just marketing) for §41's explicitly banned phrases ("Suggested Target", "Recommended Settlement", "Suggested Damage Amount", "Suggested Coverage Decision", "Suggested Liability Decision") and near-variants. This is distinct from the AI-*output* compliance already confirmed (Golden Rule #2, Bucket 1 of the 2026-08-16 audit) — this task is about static UI copy/tooltips/labels/marketing text, which has never been explicitly swept for this specific banned list.
9. **(Added 2026-08-16)** Decide whether the spec's `/api` marketing page (Page 7 — a public, sales-oriented page with a code sample) is a new page distinct from the existing `ApiDocs.jsx`/`Developers.jsx` technical reference pages, or whether those should be consolidated/relabeled instead of building a third, overlapping page.

**Files/components likely affected:** New `frontend/src/pages/Features.jsx`, `PhotoAnalysis.jsx`, `Solutions.jsx` (+ per-vertical pages), `frontend/src/pages/Home.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/Pricing.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/Security.jsx`, `frontend/src/App.jsx` (new routes), `frontend/src/components/Navbar.jsx` (nav links).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Soft dependency on Phase 2 (brand tokens, for visual consistency of new pages) and Phase 1 (so "every photo processed" claims are true by the time they're emphasized further).

**Expected output:** A complete, accurate public site matching the spec's page set and making no overstated claims (Golden Rule #1).

**Acceptance criteria:** All new pages exist, are linked from navigation, are mobile-responsive, and have basic SEO (title/meta/canonical per `CLAUDE.md` section 7); no copy on the site describes a capability that doesn't exist server-side at time of ship.

**Testing/verification steps:** Full click-through of new nav links; Lighthouse/basic SEO check on new pages; final copy audit for consistent engine naming and no overstated claims.

**Risks/considerations:** This phase is easy to scope-creep into a full site redesign — keep it strictly to adding the missing pages/sections and correcting inaccurate copy, not a broader visual overhaul (that's Phase 2's territory if pursued).

**Status:** **Completed** (2026-08-19). Implementation summary — see `TASKS.md` T-7.23 and `PROGRESS.md`'s matching changelog entry for full detail. New pages: `/features` (13 sections: hero + real screenshot + 11 feature deep-dives + CTA), `/photo-analysis` (workflow + an honestly-labeled illustrative batch/progress panel + a Golden-Rule-#2 limitations callout), `/solutions` + templated `/solutions/:slug` covering all 7 spec'd personas. Home.jsx completed: 9 feature cards, 6 How It Works steps, a new "Analyze the Entire Inspection" photo-grid/counter/progress-bar section, a "Try It Free" CTA. Engine naming standardized to **FLACRON ENGINE** sitewide, including the 3 backend files that bake the name into generated PDF/DOCX text and the AI prompt itself (`aiService.js`, `documentGenerator.js`, `properPdfGenerator.js`) — confirmed zero remaining "Flacron Engine"/"FlacronAI Engine" occurrences repo-wide. Pricing.jsx Enterprise CTA → "Talk to Sales"; tier copy already matched `tiers.js`. Security.jsx's "up to 10 images" AI-handling claim corrected to accurately describe the real 100-photos-per-report/10-per-batch behavior. Banned-terminology sweep (task 8): zero matches, confirmed clean. Task 9 (`/api` marketing page): **left untouched, explicitly blocked** — the client instructed this session that APIs should not be shown publicly and replacement documentation will be supplied; `ApiDocs.jsx`/`Developers.jsx`/their routes/nav link were not modified, removed, or hidden. Open Question #11 (Enterprise pricing model) remains unresolved, unchanged. **Verification caveat:** no real-browser (Playwright) automation was available this session, so the phase's own "full real-browser verification/Lighthouse" testing step was not performed as a genuine rendered pass — verification was lint (0 errors both packages) + build (clean, new pages code-split) + unit tests (243 backend/17 frontend passing) + manual code review + a raw-HTTP smoke test of every new route, disclosed here rather than fabricated. Flagged as a recommended follow-up once browser automation is available.

## Phase 24 — Photo Quality Warnings, Ordering, Grouping & Annotations

**Objective:** Add the remaining per-photo enrichment features the full spec calls for that aren't upload-mechanics (Phase 6) or AI-classification (Phase 8): low-quality detection, persisted ordering, area/room grouping, and drawing-based annotations.

**Reason this phase is required (2026-08-16 full-spec audit):** spec §22 (Photo Analysis feature list) wants "duplicate detection, low-quality image warning... photo ordering... captions, photo annotations"; spec Page 32 (§ Photo Documentation Layout) assumes each photo has an area/location and observation for layout purposes (partly Phase 8, partly this). **Verified via direct code search: none of low-quality detection, persisted photo ordering, room/area grouping UI, or any annotation/drawing tooling exist anywhere in the codebase.** Annotation was also a requirement in the pre-`PHASES.md` backlog (`TASKS.md` T-2.4) that was silently dropped when this roadmap was created from a summary spreadsheet audit — this phase restores it.

**Scope:** Backend: a quality-heuristic check on upload, a `position` field on photo records, a `roomOrArea` tag field. Frontend: a low-quality warning UI, drag-to-reorder in the wizard/gallery, a simple grouping/filter view, and a canvas-based annotation tool (arrows/circles/highlight/measurements/timestamps) on the full-size photo preview.

**Exact tasks:**
1. **OPEN QUESTION:** choose a low-quality heuristic approach — a cheap client/server-side check (resolution below a threshold, or a fast blur-detection algorithm like Laplacian variance via `sharp`) vs. asking the AI vision model itself to flag poor image quality as part of its analysis (only available once Phase 8's per-photo structured output exists). Recommend the cheap heuristic now (works independently of Phase 7/8), with an AI-based refinement later if wanted.
2. Add the chosen quality check to `photoBatchProcessor.js`, producing a `qualityWarning: true/false` (+ reason) field on the per-photo record; surface it as a visible warning badge in the wizard and gallery (distinct from the existing `failed`/`duplicate` statuses — a low-quality photo still uploads and analyzes, it's just flagged).
3. Add a persisted `position` (integer) field to the per-photo record; add drag-to-reorder in the wizard's grid/list view and the read-only gallery, persisting the new order via a small `PATCH /:id/photos/reorder` endpoint (or similar).
4. Add a `roomOrArea` tag (free text or, once Phase 8's taxonomy exists, a dropdown from that same list) with a simple grouping/filter view in the gallery ("show only Kitchen photos").
5. Build an annotation tool: a canvas overlay on the full-size preview (already exists as a modal in Phase 6) supporting arrows, circles/rectangles, freehand highlight, straight-line measurements, and an auto-applied capture timestamp; persist annotations as a lightweight vector/shape-list JSON alongside the photo record (never bake annotations into the stored image bytes, so they stay editable and don't corrupt the original/optimized tiers from Phase 6's addendum).

**Files/components likely affected:** `backend/utils/photoBatchProcessor.js`, `backend/routes/reports.js` (new reorder endpoint, annotation persistence), `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (wizard grid, gallery), new `frontend/src/components/PhotoAnnotator.jsx`.

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Soft dependency on Phase 6 (per-photo record must exist — it does) and Phase 8 (a real classification taxonomy makes grouping more useful, but a free-text tag works without it).

**Expected output:** Photos can be flagged for quality, manually reordered, grouped by area, and annotated — all persisting correctly.

**Acceptance criteria:** A deliberately blurry/low-resolution test photo shows a quality warning without being rejected; drag-reordering persists across a page reload; grouping by area correctly filters the gallery; an annotation (e.g. a circle + measurement) saves, reloads correctly, and does not alter the underlying image file.

**Testing/verification steps:** Upload a genuinely low-quality photo and confirm the warning; reorder photos, refresh, confirm order persisted; tag several photos by area and confirm the group filter; annotate a photo, reload the report, confirm the annotation is still there and the original/optimized/thumbnail image files are still byte-identical to before annotating.

**Risks/considerations:** Keep annotations as a separate data layer (never rasterized into the image) so Phase 6's original-preservation fix isn't undone by this phase. Canvas-based drawing tools are a real, non-trivial frontend feature — consider a lightweight existing library (e.g. `fabric.js`, `react-konva`) rather than hand-rolling canvas math, similar to Phase 9's rich-text-editor library decision.

**Status:** Completed (2026-08-19, session 27)

**Implementation summary:**
- **Open Question #1 resolved per explicit client instruction this session:** a deterministic server-side heuristic, computed on upload with `sharp` (already a dependency, no new one added) — resolution below 800×600, OR a Laplacian-variance blur score below 50 (empirically calibrated: a synthetic sharp fixture scores ~5461, the same fixture blurred scores ~32; both documented in `photoQuality.js`'s header and covered by dedicated unit tests). Never rejects a photo — `qualityWarning`/`qualityReasons`/`qualityMetrics` are purely informational, surfaced everywhere a photo appears (wizard, gallery, Photo Library). The wizard additionally gets an immediate CLIENT-side low-resolution check (from the same decode pass already used for corrupt-file detection) — blur has no cheap client equivalent, so that half only appears after the real upload/analysis pass, honestly documented as such.
- **Library decision (documented, not guessed):** `react-konva`@18.2.16 + `konva`@9.3.22 for the annotation canvas — the only React-idiomatic option compatible with this project's React 18.2 (the latest `react-konva` line requires React 19); `fabric.js` was considered and passed over for its more imperative API. `exif-reader`@2.0.3 (tiny, zero-dependency, sharp's own documented companion) was added for EXIF capture-time parsing — the only other new dependency.
- **Backend:** `photoQuality.js` (heuristic), `photoCaptureTime.js` (EXIF `DateTimeOriginal` extraction from the ORIGINAL bytes only — the display/thumbnail tiers have EXIF stripped by Phase 6's own re-encode — with a trustworthiness check rejecting clock-reset defaults like 1970/2000-01-01 and implausible future timestamps). `photoBatchProcessor.js` now stamps every uploaded photo with `position` (sequential from a caller-supplied `startPosition`, continuing correctly across `POST /:id/images` add-on batches), `qualityWarning`/`qualityReasons`/`qualityMetrics`, `capturedAt`, and initializes `roomOrArea`/`annotations` to `null`. `photoJobService.js` gained `reorderPhotos` (exact-permutation validation, no stale-check — a lost reorder is cheap to redo, unlike a lost annotation), `updatePhotoAnnotations` (full-list replace, bounded to 60 shapes/200 points-per-shape, numeric/type/color/label validation, `expectedUpdatedAt` optimistic-concurrency guarding against silently clobbering a concurrent editor), and a `set_area` branch on the existing `updatePhotoReview` (reused rather than a new route — works even when AI analysis is queued/unavailable). Two new routes: `PATCH /:id/photos/reorder`, `PUT /:id/photos/:photoId/annotations`, both on the exact dual-path authorization already established by the sibling review route (owner+`canEditReports`, or a Phase 19 `review`-tier grantee) plus the same legacy-id guard. `GET /:id/photos` and the export appendix builder (`buildAppendixPhotoList`) both now sort by `position` (falling back to array index for pre-Phase-24 photos), so the reviewer's saved order is what review, export, and the Photo Library actually see. `photoLibrary.js`/`routes/photos.js` expose the new fields and a `roomOrArea` filter (with an `__unassigned__` sentinel).
- **Frontend:** a dependency-free `useDragReorder` hook (Pointer Events, not the HTML5 Drag-and-Drop API, specifically because Pointer Events unify mouse and touch in one code path) wired into both the wizard's staged (pre-upload, client-only) grid/list and the persisted post-generation gallery; a `QualityWarningBadge` component; `PhotoAnalysisPanel` (PhotoReview.jsx) extended with a room/area select (Phase 8 taxonomy + custom free text) and an Annotate/Edit Annotations entry point, working independently of whether AI analysis exists yet; a new `PhotoAnnotator.jsx` (toolbar: Select/Move, Arrow, Rectangle, Circle, Highlight/freehand, Measurement, 5-color picker, Undo, Delete, Clear all, Save/Cancel) rendering shapes as normalized (0..1) coordinates over the real full-size image so they stay correctly positioned regardless of display size, with measurement labels always phrased as "~N px (image pixels, not a physical measurement)" — never a physical unit, since no calibration scale exists anywhere in this product — and a capture-time badge that plainly distinguishes a real EXIF timestamp from the honest upload-time fallback. Photo Library gained an area filter (taxonomy + real custom values actually present in the loaded window + an "Unassigned" option) and a "Group by Area" toggle, explicitly labeled as applying to the currently loaded page, not the whole library at once.
- **Verified end-to-end against the real Firebase/Anthropic-backed local dev stack** (both dev servers, two throwaway Firebase Auth+Firestore accounts, real Claude vision analysis, real Playwright + this machine's cached Chromium — no dedicated browser tool was available in-session, so Playwright was installed globally and driven directly via Node scripts): wizard upload with a normal/blurry/low-resolution/rotated-normal photo set, client-side low-res flagging, a real mouse-dragged wizard reorder that landed correctly at `position:0` after generation, real Claude vision analysis (which independently flagged the same synthetic images as "may be out of focus" in its own prose — an incidental cross-check that the test fixtures were realistic), server-side quality flags matching expectations exactly (blurry→`blurry`, low-res→both `low_resolution` and `blurry`, normal→neither), area tagging (taxonomy value, custom value, clearing, over-length rejection) via both direct API and the real gallery UI, persisted reorder via the API with exact-permutation and legacy-id rejection, a full annotation lifecycle (5 shapes of every type saved/persisted, a stale `expectedUpdatedAt` correctly rejected with 409, a valid one allowing an undo/delete-shrink, oversized/malformed payloads rejected with 400) with a REAL mouse-drawn arrow through the actual UI (draw → undo → redraw → save, zero console errors, zero failed requests), byte-for-byte SHA-256-verified original/display/thumbnail image bytes unchanged after annotating, Photo Library's area filter/grouping against real tagged data, full cross-account authorization isolation (a second, unrelated account got 404 on every new endpoint and never saw the first account's photos in its own library, plus a bare 401 with no token at all), and a real mobile viewport (iPhone 13 profile) pass — no horizontal overflow on the dashboard or Photo Library, the Annotate button and toolbar reachable via genuine CDP touch taps, and a drag gesture at mobile viewport size correctly drawing a shape. One test-script-only bug was found and fixed before it produced a false failure (a QA script forcibly removed the cookie-consent banner via raw DOM `.remove()`, which desynced it from React and crashed the app on next render — switched to only ever dismissing it through its own real button). A pre-existing, already-documented UX defect (the cookie-consent banner can block real clicks, including login, until dismissed) was reproduced again on the mobile login form specifically and worked around in the test script, not fixed here (unrelated to this phase). All QA accounts, the generated report, its Storage objects, and the throwaway scripts were deleted afterward; a temporary local-only rate-limit increase (100→5000 req/15min, needed only because repeated full-flow browser test runs exhausted the real limiter) was reverted to its original value before finishing — confirmed via `git diff` showing no residual change.
- **Tests:** 279/279 backend (`node --test`, 21 new: `photo-quality.test.js`, `photo-capture-time.test.js`, `photo-annotations-validation.test.js`, plus new/extended cases in `photo-batch-processor.test.js`), 0 backend lint errors (61 pre-existing warnings, unchanged). 27/27 frontend (Vitest, 10 new in `photoAnnotations.test.js`), 0 frontend lint errors (14 pre-existing warnings, unchanged), a clean production build (Konva/react-konva land in their own lazy chunk, `PhotoAnnotator-*.js` ~317KB/97KB gzip, loaded only when Dashboard or Photo Library is actually visited — no cost to the public/marketing bundle).
- **Files created:** `backend/utils/photoQuality.js`, `backend/utils/photoCaptureTime.js`, `backend/test/photo-quality.test.js`, `backend/test/photo-capture-time.test.js`, `backend/test/photo-annotations-validation.test.js`, `frontend/src/hooks/useDragReorder.js`, `frontend/src/utils/photoAnnotations.js`, `frontend/src/components/PhotoAnnotator.jsx`, `frontend/src/__tests__/photoAnnotations.test.js`.
- **Files modified:** `backend/utils/photoBatchProcessor.js`, `backend/services/photoJobService.js`, `backend/routes/reports.js`, `backend/routes/photos.js`, `backend/utils/photoLibrary.js`, `backend/test/photo-batch-processor.test.js`, `backend/package.json`/`package-lock.json` (`exif-reader`), `frontend/src/components/PhotoReview.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/PhotoLibrary.jsx`, `frontend/src/services/api.js`, `frontend/package.json`/`package-lock.json` (`konva`, `react-konva`).
- **Not done, by design:** shape resize handles after creation (move/delete/redraw only — a deliberate, disclosed scoping choice, not an oversight); a genuine on-device multi-touch drag-gesture simulation (Playwright has no high-level touch-drag primitive, and hand-constructed synthetic touch/pointer events don't trigger a real browser's touch-to-pointer synthesis the way hardware/CDP input does — real taps WERE verified via CDP, and the drag gesture itself was verified at mobile viewport size via mouse, which exercises the identical Pointer-Events-based handler code with no separate touch-only branch to miss; this is a real, disclosed tooling gap, not a product one).

## Phase 25 — Supporting Document Text Extraction & FLACRON ENGINE Integration

**Objective:** Extract text from uploaded supporting documents (PDF/DOC/DOCX/TXT) and feed it into the report-generation AI prompt, closing the gap Phase 5 deliberately left open.

**Reason this phase is required:** Phase 5's own completion notes are explicit: "Text extraction / AI-prompt integration for uploaded documents deliberately NOT implemented... Deferred to a future Phase 7/8-style follow-up." Spec §11 ("FLACRON ENGINE... Text Processing... Supporting documents") and the wizard's Step 6 ("3 Documents Processed") both assume document content actually reaches the report draft — today, documents are stored and downloadable only; their content is invisible to `generateReport()`.

**Scope:** Backend text-extraction utilities per format + AI-prompt integration; no frontend change expected beyond possibly showing "N documents processed" more accurately in the generation-progress UI.

**Exact tasks:**
1. Add text extraction per document type: PDF (a library such as `pdf-parse`), DOCX (a library such as `mammoth` or `docx` reading), TXT (already plain text — no library needed). Store extracted text on the document record (or re-extract on demand — decide based on cost/size).
2. Cap extracted text length fed into the prompt (documents can be large; avoid blowing the AI context budget) — truncate with a clear "[additional content truncated]" marker if needed.
3. Wire extracted text into `buildReportPrompt()`/`generateReport()`'s input alongside claim/inspection/photo-analysis data, clearly labeled as "from supporting document: {filename}" so the AI (and a human reviewer reading the prompt/output) can trace claims back to their source.
4. **(Optional, from the old pre-`PHASES.md` backlog, T-2.11)** Voice-to-text: allow recording a voice note during the wizard's Documentation step, transcribe it, and treat the transcript the same as a text document above — keep the original audio/transcript available for the human reviewer, never silently replace it with an AI "cleaned up" version without keeping the original.

**Files/components likely affected:** `backend/utils/documentValidation.js` (or a new sibling `documentTextExtraction.js`), `backend/services/aiService.js` (`buildReportPrompt`), `backend/routes/reports.js` (`/generate`).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** Phase 5 (document upload/storage already exists — this phase only adds the missing text-extraction/prompt-integration step).

**Expected output:** Uploaded documents' actual content measurably influences the generated report draft.

**Acceptance criteria:** Uploading a PDF containing a specific detail (e.g. a policy number, a specific damage note) and generating a report produces a draft that reflects that detail; a document that fails to extract (corrupt/password-protected/scanned-image-only PDF) degrades gracefully (report still generates, without that document's content) rather than failing the whole request.

**Testing/verification steps:** Generate a report with a real PDF/DOCX containing distinctive text, confirm it appears in the AI-generated draft; test a corrupt/password-protected document and confirm graceful degradation, not a hard failure.

**Risks/considerations:** Golden Rule #2 still applies — extracted document text is *input* to the AI, not itself a verdict; make sure the prompt framing doesn't let document content smuggle in unverified claims as if the AI generated/endorsed them. Scanned-image PDFs (no embedded text layer) will extract empty/garbage text — decide whether OCR is in scope (likely not for v1 — flag as a future enhancement if the client wants it).

**Status:** Not Started

---

## Phase 26 — Help Center & Contact Support

**Objective:** Build the `/help` Help Center and `/support` Contact Support pages.

**Reason this phase is required:** Confirmed via direct search — neither page nor route exists anywhere in the codebase, and no other phase in this roadmap mentions either one.

**Scope:** Two new frontend pages; a light backend endpoint for the Contact Support form (can likely reuse the existing sales/contact-form infrastructure pattern, e.g. `salesAPI.contact`, rather than building a parallel system).

**Exact tasks:**
1. Build `/help` with the spec's category list (Getting Started, Creating Reports, Uploading Photos, FLACRON ENGINE, Templates, Team Management, Billing, Security, API) — a simple categorized article/FAQ layout; content can start minimal (a handful of real articles per category) rather than exhaustive, to avoid this becoming a content-writing project disguised as an engineering one.
2. Build `/support` with the spec's form (Name, Email, Category, Subject, Message, Attachments) submitting to a backend endpoint that stores the request and notifies the team (reuse `emailService.js`'s existing branded-email pattern) — attachments need size/type validation matching the existing document-upload pattern (`documentValidation.js`).
3. Add both to the top bar / sidebar Help entry (spec §35) and to the public/authenticated navigation as appropriate.

**Files/components likely affected:** New `frontend/src/pages/HelpCenter.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/ContactSupport.jsx`, a new or extended backend route (e.g. `backend/routes/support.js`, or extending `backend/routes/sales.js`'s contact pattern), `frontend/src/App.jsx` (routes), `frontend/src/components/Navbar.jsx`/`Dashboard.jsx` (nav entries).

**Dependencies/prerequisites:** None blocking — this is a self-contained, additive feature.

**Expected output:** Users have a real place to find help and contact support instead of neither existing.

**Acceptance criteria:** Both pages render, are reachable from navigation, and the support form successfully creates a real, notified support request when submitted.

**Testing/verification steps:** Submit the support form with and without an attachment, confirm the request is stored/delivered; click through every Help Center category.

**Risks/considerations:** Decide the Help Center's content scope with the client up front (a handful of real articles vs. a fuller knowledge base) — this is as much a content-authoring task as an engineering one, and could balloon if not scoped.

**Status:** Not Started
